The last line is not Rein talking. It is you, running the test yourself, proving the file really changed.

What just happened

Six model calls, and a real bug fixed. Turn 2 tried to read a file outside the project, and the wall answered with an error the model could act on. Turn 3 asked for two files at once; both results went back together, each matched to its request by id. Turn 4 edited calc.py, turn 5 ran the test, and turn 6 finished.

But nothing looked at that edit before it happened. Rein would have run any command the model asked for, including one that deletes your files. That is next.

Talking point: "Every tool error is written for the model: what went wrong, and what to try next. The wall lives inside the tools, so no path gets out, whatever the model asks."

Break it on purpose

  1. In Workspace.path(), delete .resolve() from the p = ... line and run the checkpoint again. Turn 2 now reads rein.py, outside the project. Without resolve(), the path still starts with the project folder's name, so the check passes, even though ../ climbs out of it. Put .resolve() back.
  2. Change the script's old_text to just "a". The error says old_text appears 3 times. Include more lines so it is unique. Clear and actionable.
  3. Try an absolute path in the script, like /etc/hosts on Mac or Linux, or C:/Windows/win.ini on Windows. The wall holds: joining an absolute path replaces the project folder entirely, and the check catches it.
The whole rein.py after step 3 (261 lines)
"""Rein: a small coding-agent harness, built one step at a time.

Each section below is one system of the harness. Read it from top to bottom.
"""
import argparse
import json
import os
import subprocess
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path


def show(tag, text):
    """Print one line of the live transcript: line breaks become spaces, and it is cut to fit."""
    print(f"{tag:<8} {' '.join(str(text).splitlines())}"[:100])


def one_line(text):
    """Join the lines of a tool result with ' | ' for the transcript. The model still gets all of it."""
    return " | ".join(line.strip() for line in str(text).splitlines() if line.strip())


# ============ MODEL: one interface, two models ============

@dataclass
class ToolCall:
    id: str      # every request gets an id, so its result can be matched to it
    name: str    # which tool the model wants
    input: dict  # the arguments the model chose


@dataclass
class Reply:
    text: str          # what the model wrote
    tool_calls: list   # the tools it asked for (maybe none)
    stop_reason: str   # why it stopped: "end_turn", "tool_use", "max_tokens", ...
    content: list      # the raw blocks, saved into the history exactly as they came
    usage: dict = field(default_factory=dict)  # input and output token counts


class FakeModel:
    """Replays a script of replies, so every run is free, offline, and repeatable."""

    def __init__(self, replies):
        self.replies = list(replies)
        self.calls = 0

    def call(self, system, messages, tools):
        step = self.replies.pop(0) if self.replies else {"say": "(the script has run out)"}
        content, tool_calls = [], []
        if step.get("say"):
            content.append({"type": "text", "text": step["say"]})
        for name, args in step.get("tools", []):
            self.calls += 1
            call = ToolCall(f"call_{self.calls}", name, args)
            tool_calls.append(call)
            content.append({"type": "tool_use", "id": call.id, "name": name, "input": args})
        stop = step.get("stop") or ("tool_use" if tool_calls else "end_turn")
        usage = {"input_tokens": len(json.dumps(messages)) // 4,
                 "output_tokens": len(json.dumps(content)) // 4}
        return Reply(step.get("say", ""), tool_calls, stop, content, usage)


class ClaudeModel:
    """The real model, behind exactly the same interface."""

    def __init__(self, model=None):
        import anthropic
        self.client = anthropic.Anthropic()  # reads your ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
        self.model = model or os.environ.get("REIN_MODEL", "claude-sonnet-5")

    def call(self, system, messages, tools):
        extra = {"tools": tools} if tools else {}
        r = self.client.messages.create(model=self.model, max_tokens=4096,
                                        system=system, messages=messages, **extra)
        content = [block.model_dump(exclude_none=True) for block in r.content]
        return Reply(
            text="".join(b["text"] for b in content if b["type"] == "text"),
            tool_calls=[ToolCall(b["id"], b["name"], b["input"])
                        for b in content if b["type"] == "tool_use"],
            stop_reason=r.stop_reason,
            content=content,
            usage={"input_tokens": r.usage.input_tokens,
                   "output_tokens": r.usage.output_tokens},
        )


# ============ TOOLS: the model's hands ============

class ToolError(Exception):
    """An expected failure. Its message goes back to the model, so write it for the model."""


class Workspace:
    """Every path goes through here, so the agent cannot leave its project folder."""

    def __init__(self, root):
        self.root = Path(root).resolve()

    def path(self, rel):
        p = (self.root / rel).resolve()
        if p != self.root and self.root not in p.parents:
            raise ToolError(f"'{rel}' is outside the project. Use a path inside it.")
        return p


@dataclass
class Tool:
    name: str
    description: str         # the model reads this, so write it for the model
    schema: dict             # the inputs, described as JSON Schema
    run: object              # the Python function the harness calls
    read_only: bool = False  # True if the tool never changes anything

    def spec(self):
        return {"name": self.name, "description": self.description, "input_schema": self.schema}


# Commands run without writing .pyc caches, so a check never runs stale code.
CLEAN_ENV = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1"}


def make_tools(ws):
    """Four tools. Every error message tells the model what to do next."""

    def list_files():
        files = [p.relative_to(ws.root).as_posix() for p in sorted(ws.root.rglob("*")) if p.is_file()]
        return "\n".join(f for f in files if "__pycache__" not in f) or "(no files)"

    def read_file(path, start_line=1):
        p = ws.path(path)
        if not p.is_file():
            raise ToolError(f"No file at '{path}'. Call list_files to see what exists.")
        lines = p.read_text().splitlines()
        page = lines[start_line - 1:start_line + 199]
        text = "\n".join(f"{n}| {line}" for n, line in enumerate(page, start_line))
        last = start_line + len(page) - 1
        if last < len(lines):
            text += f"\n[lines {start_line}-{last} of {len(lines)}. Call again with start_line={last + 1}.]"
        return text or "(empty file)"

    def edit_file(path, old_text, new_text):
        p = ws.path(path)
        if not p.is_file():
            raise ToolError(f"No file at '{path}'. Call list_files to see what exists.")
        text = p.read_text()
        count = text.count(old_text)
        if count == 0:
            raise ToolError("old_text was not found. Read the file again: spaces must match exactly.")
        if count > 1:
            raise ToolError(f"old_text appears {count} times. Include more lines so it is unique.")
        p.write_text(text.replace(old_text, new_text))
        return f"Edited {path}."

    def run_command(command):
        try:
            r = subprocess.run(command, shell=True, cwd=ws.root, capture_output=True,
                               text=True, timeout=60, env=CLEAN_ENV)
        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
            raise ToolError("Timed out after 60 seconds. Try a smaller command.")
        return f"exit code {r.returncode}\n{(r.stdout + r.stderr).strip() or '(no output)'}"

    string = {"type": "string"}
    tools = [
        Tool("list_files", "List every file in the project. Start here.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {}}, list_files, read_only=True),
        Tool("read_file", "Read a text file with line numbers, 200 lines per page.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"path": string, "start_line": {"type": "integer"}},
              "required": ["path"]}, read_file, read_only=True),
        Tool("edit_file", "Replace one exact, unique piece of text in a file. Read the file first.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"path": string, "old_text": string, "new_text": string},
              "required": ["path", "old_text", "new_text"]}, edit_file),
        Tool("run_command", "Run a shell command in the project folder, for example the tests.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"command": string}, "required": ["command"]},
             run_command),
    ]
    return {t.name: t for t in tools}


# ============ THE LOOP ============

SYSTEM_PROMPT = ("You are Rein, a careful coding agent working inside one project folder. "
                 "Look before you change anything, and run the tests to prove your work.")


@dataclass
class Config:
    max_turns: int = 10  # hard stop: the most model calls one run may use
    workspace: str = "demo"  # the only folder the agent may touch


class Agent:
    def __init__(self, model, cfg):
        self.model, self.cfg = model, cfg
        self.tools = make_tools(Workspace(cfg.workspace))
        self.specs = [t.spec() for t in self.tools.values()]

    def say(self, tag, text):
        show(tag, text)

    def finish(self, status, note, answer=""):
        """Every way a run can end goes through here, so none of them gets forgotten."""
        self.say(status, note)
        return answer

    def run(self, task):
        messages = [{"role": "user", "content": task}]
        self.say("task", task)
        for turn in range(1, self.cfg.max_turns + 1):
            reply = self.model.call(SYSTEM_PROMPT, messages, self.specs)
            messages.append({"role": "assistant", "content": reply.content})
            self.say(f"turn {turn}", f"model: {reply.text}" if reply.text else "model asks for tools")
            if not reply.tool_calls:
                return self.finish("done", f"after {turn} model calls", reply.text)
            results = []
            for call in reply.tool_calls:
                self.say("", f"tool   {call.name} {json.dumps(call.input)}")
                output, is_error = self.run_tool(call)
                self.say("", ("error  " if is_error else "result ") + one_line(output))
                results.append({"type": "tool_result", "tool_use_id": call.id,
                                "content": output, "is_error": is_error})
            messages.append({"role": "user", "content": results})
        return self.finish("stopped", f"turn limit reached ({self.cfg.max_turns} model calls)")

    def run_tool(self, call):
        """Run one tool call. Any error becomes a message for the model, never a crash."""
        tool = self.tools.get(call.name)
        if tool is None:
            return f"Unknown tool '{call.name}'. Available tools: {', '.join(self.tools)}.", True
        try:
            return tool.run(**call.input), False
        except ToolError as e:
            return str(e), True
        except Exception as e:  # a bug, or an argument the model made up
            return f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}", True


# ============ COMMAND LINE ============

def main():
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Rein: a small coding-agent harness")
    p.add_argument("task", help="what you want done, in plain words")
    p.add_argument("--fake", metavar="SCENARIO", help="use a scripted model from this JSON file")
    p.add_argument("--workspace", default="demo", help="the project folder the agent works in")
    args = p.parse_args()

    if args.fake:
        with open(args.fake) as f:
            scenario = json.load(f)
        model = FakeModel(scenario["replies"])
    else:
        model = ClaudeModel()

    cfg = Config(
        workspace=args.workspace,
    )
    Agent(model, cfg).run(args.task)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

4 The permission gate

GoalEvery tool call gets a verdict before it runs: allow, ask you, or deny, with a reason the model can read. TheoryChapter 12: permissions and sandboxes Sizerein.py grows to 317 lines

4aOne import

re is Python's module for regular expressions, which Rein uses to match command patterns.

rein.py+1 −0
at the top of the file
import jsonimport osimport reimport subprocessfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field

4bThe policy

The whole policy is one function, decide(), and it checks things in a fixed order:

  1. Read-only tools are always allowed. Looking cannot break anything.
  2. Commands are checked against BLOCKED first. Deny rules beat allow rules, so a command that matches both is denied.
  3. A command on the ALLOWED list runs without asking, but only if it has no chaining characters. Otherwise python test_calc.py && curl evil.example would slip through, because it starts like an allowed command.
  4. Every other command asks you.
  5. File edits ask you, unless you chose --mode auto.

Every Verdict carries a reason, and the model reads it, so the model can change course instead of trying the same thing again.

rein.py+34 −0
new code
# ============ PERMISSIONS: allow, ask, or deny ============BLOCKED = [r"\brm\s", r"\bsudo\b", r"\bgit\s+push\b", r"\bcurl\b", r"\bwget\b"]  # neverALLOWED = [r"^python\s+test_\w+\.py\b", r"^python\s+-m\s+pytest\b"]            # always fineCHAINING = re.compile(r"[;&|`$<>]")  # an allowed command must not smuggle in a second one@dataclassclass Verdict:    decision: str  # "allow", "ask", or "deny"    reason: str    # the model reads this too, so say whydef decide(tool, args, mode):    """The policy. Deny rules are checked before allow rules."""    if tool.read_only:        return Verdict("allow", "read-only tool")    if tool.name == "run_command":        command = args.get("command", "").strip()        if any(re.search(p, command) for p in BLOCKED):            return Verdict("deny", "destructive, network, or admin command")        if any(re.search(p, command) for p in ALLOWED) and not CHAINING.search(command):            return Verdict("allow", "allow-listed command")        return Verdict("ask", "this command is not on the allow-list")    if mode == "auto":        return Verdict("allow", "edits are allowed in auto mode")    return Verdict("ask", "this tool changes files")def ask_human(call, reason):    answer = input(f"         ? allow {call.name}? ({reason}) [y/N] ")    return answer.strip().lower() == "y"# ============ THE LOOP ============

4cWire it in

run_tool() now asks the gate before it runs anything, and prints the verdict. check_permission() adds two rules on top of decide(). An "ask" becomes allow or deny, depending on your answer. And a denied call stays denied for the rest of the run, so the model cannot wear you down by asking again.

in class Config

in Agent.__init__()
        self.tools = make_tools(Workspace(cfg.workspace))        self.specs = [t.spec() for t in self.tools.values()]        self.denied = set()  # once denied, always denied    def say(self, tag, text):
in Agent.run_tool()
        if tool is None:            return f"Unknown tool '{call.name}'. Available tools: {', '.join(self.tools)}.", True        verdict = self.check_permission(tool, call)        self.say("", f"{verdict.decision:<6} {verdict.reason}")        if verdict.decision == "deny":            return f"Permission denied: {verdict.reason}. Do not retry this; find another way.", True        try:            return tool.run(**call.input), False
new method in class Agent
        except Exception as e:  # a bug, or an argument the model made up            return f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}", True    def check_permission(self, tool, call):        key = call.name + json.dumps(call.input, sort_keys=True)        if key in self.denied:            verdict = Verdict("deny", "this exact call was already denied")        else:            verdict = decide(tool, call.input, self.cfg.mode)        if verdict.decision == "ask":            yes = ask_human(call, verdict.reason)            verdict = Verdict("allow", "a human said yes") if yes else Verdict("deny", "a human said no")        if verdict.decision == "deny":            self.denied.add(key)        return verdict

The script for this step

Checkpoint: step 4

When Rein asks about the edit, type y and press Enter.

python reset_demo.py
python rein.py "Fix the failing test in calc.py" --fake scenarios/04_gate.json
Demo project ready: demo/calc.py, demo/test_calc.py, demo/app.log, demo/AGENTS.md
task     Fix the failing test in calc.py
turn 1   model: First I will clean up old build files.
         tool   run_command {"command": "rm -rf build"}
         deny   destructive, network, or admin command
         error  Permission denied: destructive, network, or admin command. Do not retry this; find a
turn 2   model: That was denied, so I will skip the cleanup.
         tool   read_file {"path": "calc.py"}
         allow  read-only tool
         result 1| def add(a, b): | 2|     return a - b
turn 3   model: Fixing the bug.
         tool   edit_file {"path": "calc.py", "old_text": "return a - b", "new_text": "return a + b"
         ? allow edit_file? (this tool changes files) [y/N] y
         allow  a human said yes
         result Edited calc.py.
turn 4   model: Running the test.
         tool   run_command {"command": "python test_calc.py"}
         allow  allow-listed command
         result exit code 0 | 1 test passed
turn 5   model: Fixed add() in calc.py. The test passes.
done     after 5 model calls

What just happened

The rm -rf was denied before it could run, and the reason went back to the model, which changed course. The read was allowed without a question, because reading is safe. The edit stopped and asked you. The test command was on the allow-list, so it simply ran.

Someone determined can always find a command your patterns miss. The real boundary is a sandbox. For anything beyond practice folders, run Rein in a container that can only see the build folder and has no network:

docker run --rm -it --network none -v "$PWD:/app" -w /app python:3.12-slim \
  python rein.py "Fix the failing test in calc.py" --fake scenarios/03_fix.json --mode auto

With --network none the container cannot reach the API either, so this runs the scripted model. For real runs, managed agents keep the loop outside the container and run only the tools inside it.

Break it on purpose

  1. Run the checkpoint again, but answer n. The edit is denied and the test fails with exit code 1. Yet the model's last line still claims "The test passes", and the run ends as done. Nothing checks that claim yet. Step 9 will.
  2. Change the test command in the script to python test_calc.py && echo ok. Rein asks instead of allowing it. Then try python test_calc.py; rm -rf demo: denied outright, because deny rules come first.
  3. Run with --mode auto. The edit goes through without a question. Which mode would you give an agent that works unattended at night, and what else would you want around it?

The model asks for read_file with the path ../rein.py. The gate says allow, because read_file is read-only. What stops the read?

  • Nothing. The file gets read.
  • The workspace wall: the tool itself checks every path.
  • The model's system prompt.

The gate and the wall are separate layers. The gate decides whether a kind of action is allowed; the wall, inside the tools, decides which files any action can touch. You will see both in the next checkpoint.

The whole rein.py after step 4 (317 lines)

Each section below is one system of the harness. Read it from top to bottom.

                               text=True, timeout=60, env=CLEAN_ENV)
        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
            raise ToolError("Timed out after 60 seconds. Try a smaller command.")
        return f"exit code {r.returncode}\n{(r.stdout + r.stderr).strip() or '(no output)'}"

    string = {"type": "string"}
    tools = [
        Tool("list_files", "List every file in the project. Start here.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {}}, list_files, read_only=True),
        Tool("read_file", "Read a text file with line numbers, 200 lines per page.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"path": string, "start_line": {"type": "integer"}},
              "required": ["path"]}, read_file, read_only=True),
        Tool("edit_file", "Replace one exact, unique piece of text in a file. Read the file first.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"path": string, "old_text": string, "new_text": string},
              "required": ["path", "old_text", "new_text"]}, edit_file),
        Tool("run_command", "Run a shell command in the project folder, for example the tests.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"command": string}, "required": ["command"]},
             run_command),
    ]
    return {t.name: t for t in tools}


# ============ PERMISSIONS: allow, ask, or deny ============

BLOCKED = [r"\brm\s", r"\bsudo\b", r"\bgit\s+push\b", r"\bcurl\b", r"\bwget\b"]  # never
ALLOWED = [r"^python\s+test_\w+\.py\b", r"^python\s+-m\s+pytest\b"]            # always fine
CHAINING = re.compile(r"[;&|`$<>]")  # an allowed command must not smuggle in a second one


@dataclass
class Verdict:
    decision: str  # "allow", "ask", or "deny"
    reason: str    # the model reads this too, so say why


def decide(tool, args, mode):
    """The policy. Deny rules are checked before allow rules."""
    if tool.read_only:
        return Verdict("allow", "read-only tool")
    if tool.name == "run_command":
        command = args.get("command", "").strip()
        if any(re.search(p, command) for p in BLOCKED):
            return Verdict("deny", "destructive, network, or admin command")
        if any(re.search(p, command) for p in ALLOWED) and not CHAINING.search(command):
            return Verdict("allow", "allow-listed command")
        return Verdict("ask", "this command is not on the allow-list")
    if mode == "auto":
        return Verdict("allow", "edits are allowed in auto mode")
    return Verdict("ask", "this tool changes files")


def ask_human(call, reason):
    answer = input(f"         ? allow {call.name}? ({reason}) [y/N] ")
    return answer.strip().lower() == "y"


# ============ THE LOOP ============

SYSTEM_PROMPT = ("You are Rein, a careful coding agent working inside one project folder. "
                 "Look before you change anything, and run the tests to prove your work.")


@dataclass
class Config:
    max_turns: int = 10  # hard stop: the most model calls one run may use
    workspace: str = "demo"  # the only folder the agent may touch
    mode: str = "ask"        # "ask": edits need a human yes. "auto": edits are allowed


class Agent:
    def __init__(self, model, cfg):
        self.model, self.cfg = model, cfg
        self.tools = make_tools(Workspace(cfg.workspace))
        self.specs = [t.spec() for t in self.tools.values()]
        self.denied = set()  # once denied, always denied

    def say(self, tag, text):
        show(tag, text)

    def finish(self, status, note, answer=""):
        """Every way a run can end goes through here, so none of them gets forgotten."""
        self.say(status, note)
        return answer

    def run(self, task):
        messages = [{"role": "user", "content": task}]
        self.say("task", task)
        for turn in range(1, self.cfg.max_turns + 1):
            reply = self.model.call(SYSTEM_PROMPT, messages, self.specs)
            messages.append({"role": "assistant", "content": reply.content})
            self.say(f"turn {turn}", f"model: {reply.text}" if reply.text else "model asks for tools")
            if not reply.tool_calls:
                return self.finish("done", f"after {turn} model calls", reply.text)
            results = []
            for call in reply.tool_calls:
                self.say("", f"tool   {call.name} {json.dumps(call.input)}")
                output, is_error = self.run_tool(call)
                self.say("", ("error  " if is_error else "result ") + one_line(output))
                results.append({"type": "tool_result", "tool_use_id": call.id,
                                "content": output, "is_error": is_error})
            messages.append({"role": "user", "content": results})
        return self.finish("stopped", f"turn limit reached ({self.cfg.max_turns} model calls)")

    def run_tool(self, call):
        """Run one tool call. Any error becomes a message for the model, never a crash."""
        tool = self.tools.get(call.name)
        if tool is None:
            return f"Unknown tool '{call.name}'. Available tools: {', '.join(self.tools)}.", True
        verdict = self.check_permission(tool, call)
        self.say("", f"{verdict.decision:<6} {verdict.reason}")
        if verdict.decision == "deny":
            return f"Permission denied: {verdict.reason}. Do not retry this; find another way.", True
        try:
            return tool.run(**call.input), False
        except ToolError as e:
            return str(e), True
        except Exception as e:  # a bug, or an argument the model made up
            return f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}", True

    def check_permission(self, tool, call):
        key = call.name + json.dumps(call.input, sort_keys=True)
        if key in self.denied:
            verdict = Verdict("deny", "this exact call was already denied")
        else:
            verdict = decide(tool, call.input, self.cfg.mode)
        if verdict.decision == "ask":
            yes = ask_human(call, verdict.reason)
            verdict = Verdict("allow", "a human said yes") if yes else Verdict("deny", "a human said no")
        if verdict.decision == "deny":
            self.denied.add(key)
        return verdict


# ============ COMMAND LINE ============

def main():
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Rein: a small coding-agent harness")
    p.add_argument("task", help="what you want done, in plain words")
    p.add_argument("--fake", metavar="SCENARIO", help="use a scripted model from this JSON file")
    p.add_argument("--workspace", default="demo", help="the project folder the agent works in")
    p.add_argument("--mode", default="ask", choices=["ask", "auto"], help="auto: allow edits without asking")
    args = p.parse_args()

    if args.fake:
        with open(args.fake) as f:
            scenario = json.load(f)
        model = FakeModel(scenario["replies"])
    else:
        model = ClaudeModel()

    cfg = Config(
        workspace=args.workspace,
        mode=args.mode,
    )
    Agent(model, cfg).run(args.task)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Carry this forward

  • The wall lives inside the tools: every path is resolved, then checked.
  • Tool errors are written for the model: what went wrong, and what to try next.
  • Every call gets a verdict with a reason: allow, ask, or deny. Deny rules come first.
  • Pattern lists are a convenience. The real boundary is a sandbox.

Steps 5 and 6: a trace, then a budget

Rein starts keeping a record of everything it does, so you can see inside any run. Then it learns to keep the conversation inside a budget: long tool results get cut, and old history gets summarized.

5 A trace of everything

GoalRecord every model call, verdict, and tool call in a file as it happens, and print a summary at the end. TheoryChapter 18: observability Sizerein.py grows to 350 lines

5aA clock

time lets Rein note when each event happened.

rein.py+1 −0
at the top of the file
import reimport subprocessimport timefrom dataclasses import dataclass, fieldfrom pathlib import Path

5bThe tracer

The Tracer writes one line of JSON for every event, the moment it happens. That format is called JSONL. Because each line is written straight away, a crash halfway through loses nothing that came before it. Each record says when it happened (t, in seconds since the start), what kind of event it was, and any details.

summary() counts the events by kind and adds up the tokens. It counts whatever kinds exist, so the events you add in later steps show up in the summary without any extra code.

rein.py+24 −0
new code
# ============ TRACE: a record of everything ============class Tracer:    """Writes one JSON object per event to a file (JSONL), and keeps them for a summary."""    def __init__(self, path):        self.path, self.start, self.events = path, time.time(), []        Path(path).write_text("")  # a fresh trace file for every run    def event(self, kind, **data):        record = {"t": round(time.time() - self.start, 3), "kind": kind, **data}        self.events.append(record)        with open(self.path, "a") as f:            f.write(json.dumps(record) + "\n")    def summary(self):        counts = {}        for e in self.events:            if e["kind"] not in ("run_start", "run_end"):                counts[e["kind"]] = counts.get(e["kind"], 0) + 1        tokens = sum(e.get("input_tokens", 0) + e.get("output_tokens", 0) for e in self.events)        return ", ".join(f"{n} {kind}" for kind, n in counts.items()) + f", {tokens} tokens"# ============ THE LOOP ============

5cWire it in

The agent now takes a tracer and records five kinds of events: run_start; model_call, with the token counts; permission, with the verdict and its reason; tool_call, with whether it failed and how long the output was; and run_end, with how the run ended. main() prints the summary at the end.

What just happened

The same six turns as in step 3, now with a verdict on every tool call and a summary on the last line: 6 model calls, 6 permission checks, 6 tool calls, and 1,993 tokens, as estimated by the scripted model.

Look at turn 2 again. The gate allowed the read, because read_file is read-only, and then the workspace wall still blocked it. Two separate layers, each doing its own job. Now open trace.jsonl in your editor. Every line is one event:

{"t": 0.0, "kind": "run_start", "task": "Fix the failing test in calc.py"}
{"t": 0.0, "kind": "model_call", "turn": 1, "stop_reason": "tool_use", "input_tokens": 16, "output_tokens": 32}
{"t": 0.0, "kind": "permission", "tool": "list_files", "decision": "allow", "reason": "read-only tool"}
{"t": 0.001, "kind": "tool_call", "tool": "list_files", "error": false, "chars": 38}
{"t": 0.001, "kind": "model_call", "turn": 2, "stop_reason": "tool_use", "input_tokens": 96, "output_tokens": 23}

Talking point: "Every model call, verdict, retry, and compaction is an event in a JSONL trace, written as it happens. The summary is generic, so new behavior shows up in it without new code."

Break it on purpose

  1. Find the one tool call in trace.jsonl with "error": true. Which turn was it, and why did it fail?
  2. Add self.trace.event("note", text="hello") as the first line of run() and run the checkpoint again. The summary now includes 1 note, and you did not touch summary().
  3. Why does event() open and close the file for every single event, instead of writing everything once at the end? Think about what the trace would hold if Rein crashed in turn 4.
The whole rein.py after step 5 (350 lines)
"""Rein: a small coding-agent harness, built one step at a time.

Each section below is one system of the harness. Read it from top to bottom.
"""
import argparse
import json
import os
import re
import subprocess
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path


def show(tag, text):
    """Print one line of the live transcript: line breaks become spaces, and it is cut to fit."""
    print(f"{tag:<8} {' '.join(str(text).splitlines())}"[:100])


def one_line(text):
    """Join the lines of a tool result with ' | ' for the transcript. The model still gets all of it."""
    return " | ".join(line.strip() for line in str(text).splitlines() if line.strip())


# ============ MODEL: one interface, two models ============

@dataclass
class ToolCall:
    id: str      # every request gets an id, so its result can be matched to it
    name: str    # which tool the model wants
    input: dict  # the arguments the model chose


@dataclass
class Reply:
    text: str          # what the model wrote
    tool_calls: list   # the tools it asked for (maybe none)
    stop_reason: str   # why it stopped: "end_turn", "tool_use", "max_tokens", ...
    content: list      # the raw blocks, saved into the history exactly as they came
    usage: dict = field(default_factory=dict)  # input and output token counts


class FakeModel:
    """Replays a script of replies, so every run is free, offline, and repeatable."""

    def __init__(self, replies):
        self.replies = list(replies)
        self.calls = 0

    def call(self, system, messages, tools):
        step = self.replies.pop(0) if self.replies else {"say": "(the script has run out)"}
        content, tool_calls = [], []
        if step.get("say"):
            content.append({"type": "text", "text": step["say"]})
        for name, args in step.get("tools", []):
            self.calls += 1
            call = ToolCall(f"call_{self.calls}", name, args)
            tool_calls.append(call)
            content.append({"type": "tool_use", "id": call.id, "name": name, "input": args})
        stop = step.get("stop") or ("tool_use" if tool_calls else "end_turn")
        usage = {"input_tokens": len(json.dumps(messages)) // 4,
                 "output_tokens": len(json.dumps(content)) // 4}
        return Reply(step.get("say", ""), tool_calls, stop, content, usage)


class ClaudeModel:
    """The real model, behind exactly the same interface."""

    def __init__(self, model=None):
        import anthropic
        self.client = anthropic.Anthropic()  # reads your ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
        self.model = model or os.environ.get("REIN_MODEL", "claude-sonnet-5")

    def call(self, system, messages, tools):
        extra = {"tools": tools} if tools else {}
        r = self.client.messages.create(model=self.model, max_tokens=4096,
                                        system=system, messages=messages, **extra)
        content = [block.model_dump(exclude_none=True) for block in r.content]
        return Reply(
            text="".join(b["text"] for b in content if b["type"] == "text"),
            tool_calls=[ToolCall(b["id"], b["name"], b["input"])
                        for b in content if b["type"] == "tool_use"],
            stop_reason=r.stop_reason,
            content=content,
            usage={"input_tokens": r.usage.input_tokens,
                   "output_tokens": r.usage.output_tokens},
        )


# ============ TOOLS: the model's hands ============

class ToolError(Exception):
    """An expected failure. Its message goes back to the model, so write it for the model."""


class Workspace:
    """Every path goes through here, so the agent cannot leave its project folder."""

    def __init__(self, root):
        self.root = Path(root).resolve()

    def path(self, rel):
        p = (self.root / rel).resolve()
        if p != self.root and self.root not in p.parents:
            raise ToolError(f"'{rel}' is outside the project. Use a path inside it.")
        return p


@dataclass
class Tool:
    name: str
    description: str         # the model reads this, so write it for the model
    schema: dict             # the inputs, described as JSON Schema
    run: object              # the Python function the harness calls
    read_only: bool = False  # True if the tool never changes anything

    def spec(self):
        return {"name": self.name, "description": self.description, "input_schema": self.schema}


# Commands run without writing .pyc caches, so a check never runs stale code.
CLEAN_ENV = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1"}


def make_tools(ws):
    """Four tools. Every error message tells the model what to do next."""

    def list_files():
        files = [p.relative_to(ws.root).as_posix() for p in sorted(ws.root.rglob("*")) if p.is_file()]
        return "\n".join(f for f in files if "__pycache__" not in f) or "(no files)"

    def read_file(path, start_line=1):
        p = ws.path(path)
        if not p.is_file():
            raise ToolError(f"No file at '{path}'. Call list_files to see what exists.")
        lines = p.read_text().splitlines()
        page = lines[start_line - 1:start_line + 199]
        text = "\n".join(f"{n}| {line}" for n, line in enumerate(page, start_line))
        last = start_line + len(page) - 1
        if last < len(lines):
            text += f"\n[lines {start_line}-{last} of {len(lines)}. Call again with start_line={last + 1}.]"
        return text or "(empty file)"

    def edit_file(path, old_text, new_text):
        p = ws.path(path)
        if not p.is_file():
            raise ToolError(f"No file at '{path}'. Call list_files to see what exists.")
        text = p.read_text()
        count = text.count(old_text)
        if count == 0:
            raise ToolError("old_text was not found. Read the file again: spaces must match exactly.")
        if count > 1:
            raise ToolError(f"old_text appears {count} times. Include more lines so it is unique.")
        p.write_text(text.replace(old_text, new_text))
        return f"Edited {path}."

    def run_command(command):
        try:
            r = subprocess.run(command, shell=True, cwd=ws.root, capture_output=True,
                               text=True, timeout=60, env=CLEAN_ENV)
        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
            raise ToolError("Timed out after 60 seconds. Try a smaller command.")
        return f"exit code {r.returncode}\n{(r.stdout + r.stderr).strip() or '(no output)'}"

    string = {"type": "string"}
    tools = [
        Tool("list_files", "List every file in the project. Start here.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {}}, list_files, read_only=True),
        Tool("read_file", "Read a text file with line numbers, 200 lines per page.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"path": string, "start_line": {"type": "integer"}},
              "required": ["path"]}, read_file, read_only=True),
        Tool("edit_file", "Replace one exact, unique piece of text in a file. Read the file first.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"path": string, "old_text": string, "new_text": string},
              "required": ["path", "old_text", "new_text"]}, edit_file),
        Tool("run_command", "Run a shell command in the project folder, for example the tests.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"command": string}, "required": ["command"]},
             run_command),
    ]
    return {t.name: t for t in tools}


# ============ PERMISSIONS: allow, ask, or deny ============

BLOCKED = [r"\brm\s", r"\bsudo\b", r"\bgit\s+push\b", r"\bcurl\b", r"\bwget\b"]  # never
ALLOWED = [r"^python\s+test_\w+\.py\b", r"^python\s+-m\s+pytest\b"]            # always fine
CHAINING = re.compile(r"[;&|`$<>]")  # an allowed command must not smuggle in a second one


@dataclass
class Verdict:
    decision: str  # "allow", "ask", or "deny"
    reason: str    # the model reads this too, so say why


def decide(tool, args, mode):
    """The policy. Deny rules are checked before allow rules."""
    if tool.read_only:
        return Verdict("allow", "read-only tool")
    if tool.name == "run_command":
        command = args.get("command", "").strip()
        if any(re.search(p, command) for p in BLOCKED):
            return Verdict("deny", "destructive, network, or admin command")
        if any(re.search(p, command) for p in ALLOWED) and not CHAINING.search(command):
            return Verdict("allow", "allow-listed command")
        return Verdict("ask", "this command is not on the allow-list")
    if mode == "auto":
        return Verdict("allow", "edits are allowed in auto mode")
    return Verdict("ask", "this tool changes files")


def ask_human(call, reason):
    answer = input(f"         ? allow {call.name}? ({reason}) [y/N] ")
    return answer.strip().lower() == "y"


# ============ TRACE: a record of everything ============

class Tracer:
    """Writes one JSON object per event to a file (JSONL), and keeps them for a summary."""

    def __init__(self, path):
        self.path, self.start, self.events = path, time.time(), []
        Path(path).write_text("")  # a fresh trace file for every run

    def event(self, kind, **data):
        record = {"t": round(time.time() - self.start, 3), "kind": kind, **data}
        self.events.append(record)
        with open(self.path, "a") as f:
            f.write(json.dumps(record) + "\n")

    def summary(self):
        counts = {}
        for e in self.events:
            if e["kind"] not in ("run_start", "run_end"):
                counts[e["kind"]] = counts.get(e["kind"], 0) + 1
        tokens = sum(e.get("input_tokens", 0) + e.get("output_tokens", 0) for e in self.events)
        return ", ".join(f"{n} {kind}" for kind, n in counts.items()) + f", {tokens} tokens"


# ============ THE LOOP ============

SYSTEM_PROMPT = ("You are Rein, a careful coding agent working inside one project folder. "
                 "Look before you change anything, and run the tests to prove your work.")


@dataclass
class Config:
    max_turns: int = 10  # hard stop: the most model calls one run may use
    workspace: str = "demo"  # the only folder the agent may touch
    mode: str = "ask"        # "ask": edits need a human yes. "auto": edits are allowed
    trace_file: str = "trace.jsonl"  # every event of the run is recorded here


class Agent:
    def __init__(self, model, cfg, trace):
        self.model, self.cfg, self.trace = model, cfg, trace
        self.tools = make_tools(Workspace(cfg.workspace))
        self.specs = [t.spec() for t in self.tools.values()]
        self.denied = set()  # once denied, always denied

    def say(self, tag, text):
        show(tag, text)

    def finish(self, status, note, answer=""):
        """Every way a run can end goes through here, so none of them gets forgotten."""
        self.say(status, note)
        self.trace.event("run_end", status=status)
        return answer

    def run(self, task):
        messages = [{"role": "user", "content": task}]
        self.say("task", task)
        self.trace.event("run_start", task=task)
        for turn in range(1, self.cfg.max_turns + 1):
            reply = self.model.call(SYSTEM_PROMPT, messages, self.specs)
            self.trace.event("model_call", turn=turn, stop_reason=reply.stop_reason, **reply.usage)
            messages.append({"role": "assistant", "content": reply.content})
            self.say(f"turn {turn}", f"model: {reply.text}" if reply.text else "model asks for tools")
            if not reply.tool_calls:
                return self.finish("done", f"after {turn} model calls", reply.text)
            results = []
            for call in reply.tool_calls:
                self.say("", f"tool   {call.name} {json.dumps(call.input)}")
                output, is_error = self.run_tool(call)
                self.trace.event("tool_call", tool=call.name, error=is_error, chars=len(output))
                self.say("", ("error  " if is_error else "result ") + one_line(output))
                results.append({"type": "tool_result", "tool_use_id": call.id,
                                "content": output, "is_error": is_error})
            messages.append({"role": "user", "content": results})
        return self.finish("stopped", f"turn limit reached ({self.cfg.max_turns} model calls)")

    def run_tool(self, call):
        """Run one tool call. Any error becomes a message for the model, never a crash."""
        tool = self.tools.get(call.name)
        if tool is None:
            return f"Unknown tool '{call.name}'. Available tools: {', '.join(self.tools)}.", True
        verdict = self.check_permission(tool, call)
        self.say("", f"{verdict.decision:<6} {verdict.reason}")
        if verdict.decision == "deny":
            return f"Permission denied: {verdict.reason}. Do not retry this; find another way.", True
        try:
            return tool.run(**call.input), False
        except ToolError as e:
            return str(e), True
        except Exception as e:  # a bug, or an argument the model made up
            return f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}", True

    def check_permission(self, tool, call):
        key = call.name + json.dumps(call.input, sort_keys=True)
        if key in self.denied:
            verdict = Verdict("deny", "this exact call was already denied")
        else:
            verdict = decide(tool, call.input, self.cfg.mode)
        if verdict.decision == "ask":
            yes = ask_human(call, verdict.reason)
            verdict = Verdict("allow", "a human said yes") if yes else Verdict("deny", "a human said no")
        if verdict.decision == "deny":
            self.denied.add(key)
        self.trace.event("permission", tool=call.name, decision=verdict.decision, reason=verdict.reason)
        return verdict


# ============ COMMAND LINE ============

def main():
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Rein: a small coding-agent harness")
    p.add_argument("task", help="what you want done, in plain words")
    p.add_argument("--fake", metavar="SCENARIO", help="use a scripted model from this JSON file")
    p.add_argument("--workspace", default="demo", help="the project folder the agent works in")
    p.add_argument("--mode", default="ask", choices=["ask", "auto"], help="auto: allow edits without asking")
    args = p.parse_args()

    if args.fake:
        with open(args.fake) as f:
            scenario = json.load(f)
        model = FakeModel(scenario["replies"])
    else:
        model = ClaudeModel()

    cfg = Config(
        workspace=args.workspace,
        mode=args.mode,
    )
    trace = Tracer(cfg.trace_file)
    Agent(model, cfg, trace).run(args.task)
    show("trace", f"{trace.summary()} (details in {cfg.trace_file})")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

6 A budget for the context

GoalCap every tool result, and summarize old history when the conversation grows past a limit. TheoryChapter 13: the context budget and chapter 14: compaction Sizerein.py grows to 395 lines

6aThree small functions

  • estimate_tokens() guesses the size of the conversation at about four characters per token. It is rough, but good enough to decide when to act.
  • truncate() cuts a result that is too long. It keeps the head and the tail, and says how much it cut. In logs and test output, the error is usually near the end, so the tail matters most.
  • compact() swaps old history for a short summary and keeps the newest messages word for word. The cut always lands on one of the model's messages, so a tool request is never separated from its result. If there is nothing old enough to summarize yet, it returns None.

6bWire it in

Before each model call, the loop estimates the size of the conversation and compacts it if it is over the limit. messages[:] = smaller replaces what is inside the list instead of making a new list, so anything else holding the same list sees the change. Step 8 depends on that. run_tool() now cuts long results down to tool_output_chars, and prints a cut line when it does. --context-limit lets you set the limit from the command line.

Checkpoint: step 6

The limit of 1,200 tokens is tiny on purpose, so you can watch compaction happen. The real default is 20,000.

python reset_demo.py
python rein.py "Find the error in app.log" --fake scenarios/06_context.json --context-limit 1200
Demo project ready: demo/calc.py, demo/test_calc.py, demo/app.log, demo/AGENTS.md

turn 1   model: The log is long. I will read the first page.

turn 2   model: The middle was cut. Errors are usually near the end.
         tool   read_file {"path": "app.log", "start_line": 201}

         cut    11464 characters down to 2000
         result 201| 2026-09-26 10:03:21 INFO request 201 served in 10 ms | 202| 2026-09-26 10:03:22
turn 3   model: Line 397 has the error. Let me look at the code it names.

         allow  read-only tool
         result 1| from calc import add | 2| | 3| | 4| def test_add(): | 5|     result = add(2, 3) |
turn 5   model: Found it: add(2, 3) returned -1 because add() subtracts. That is the bug to fix.
done     after 5 model calls
trace    5 model_call, 4 permission, 4 tool_call, 1 compact, 3709 tokens (details in trace.jsonl)

What just happened

The first page of the log was 11,293 characters, and the model got 2,000 of them: the head and the tail, with a note in the middle saying what was cut. The model saw the cut and asked for page 2, where the tail showed the error at line 397.

Before turn 3, the conversation was already just over the limit, but there was nothing old enough to summarize, so Rein left it alone. Before turn 4 it was about 1,310 tokens, so Rein compacted it to about 751, and the model carried on without the first page.

This compaction is mechanical: it cannot invent anything, and it cannot fail. A summary written by a model is smarter, but it costs a call and can leave out or get wrong the one detail that mattered. Serious harnesses often use both: a model-written summary, with a mechanical fallback.

Break it on purpose

  1. Run the checkpoint with --context-limit 100. Rein now compacts before every turn where there is something old enough to summarize. What does the model lose each time?
  2. In truncate(), keep only the head: return text[:max_chars]. Run the checkpoint again and look at what the model receives from page 2: the error at line 397 is gone. The scripted model still "finds" it, because its script is fixed. A real model would miss it. Put the tail back.
  3. Why must compact() never cut right after a tool request? Find the line that prevents it, and say what the API would do with a request that has no result.

Why does truncate() keep the tail of a long output, and not just the head?

  • The tail is always shorter.
  • Errors and final results tend to appear at the end of logs and test output.
  • The API rejects outputs that do not end the way they started.

The start of a log is often routine and the failure comes last. Keeping the head and the tail gives the model the setup and the verdict.

The whole rein.py after step 6 (395 lines)
"""Rein: a small coding-agent harness, built one step at a time.

Each section below is one system of the harness. Read it from top to bottom.
"""
import argparse
import json
import os
import re
import subprocess
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path


def show(tag, text):
    """Print one line of the live transcript: line breaks become spaces, and it is cut to fit."""
    print(f"{tag:<8} {' '.join(str(text).splitlines())}"[:100])


def one_line(text):
    """Join the lines of a tool result with ' | ' for the transcript. The model still gets all of it."""
    return " | ".join(line.strip() for line in str(text).splitlines() if line.strip())


# ============ MODEL: one interface, two models ============

@dataclass
class ToolCall:
    id: str      # every request gets an id, so its result can be matched to it
    name: str    # which tool the model wants
    input: dict  # the arguments the model chose


@dataclass
class Reply:
    text: str          # what the model wrote
    tool_calls: list   # the tools it asked for (maybe none)
    stop_reason: str   # why it stopped: "end_turn", "tool_use", "max_tokens", ...
    content: list      # the raw blocks, saved into the history exactly as they came
    usage: dict = field(default_factory=dict)  # input and output token counts


class FakeModel:
    """Replays a script of replies, so every run is free, offline, and repeatable."""

    def __init__(self, replies):
        self.replies = list(replies)
        self.calls = 0

    def call(self, system, messages, tools):
        step = self.replies.pop(0) if self.replies else {"say": "(the script has run out)"}
        content, tool_calls = [], []
        if step.get("say"):
            content.append({"type": "text", "text": step["say"]})
        for name, args in step.get("tools", []):
            self.calls += 1
            call = ToolCall(f"call_{self.calls}", name, args)
            tool_calls.append(call)
            content.append({"type": "tool_use", "id": call.id, "name": name, "input": args})
        stop = step.get("stop") or ("tool_use" if tool_calls else "end_turn")
        usage = {"input_tokens": len(json.dumps(messages)) // 4,
                 "output_tokens": len(json.dumps(content)) // 4}
        return Reply(step.get("say", ""), tool_calls, stop, content, usage)


class ClaudeModel:
    """The real model, behind exactly the same interface."""

    def __init__(self, model=None):
        import anthropic
        self.client = anthropic.Anthropic()  # reads your ANTHROPIC_API_KEY
        self.model = model or os.environ.get("REIN_MODEL", "claude-sonnet-5")

    def call(self, system, messages, tools):
        extra = {"tools": tools} if tools else {}
        r = self.client.messages.create(model=self.model, max_tokens=4096,
                                        system=system, messages=messages, **extra)
        content = [block.model_dump(exclude_none=True) for block in r.content]
        return Reply(
            text="".join(b["text"] for b in content if b["type"] == "text"),
            tool_calls=[ToolCall(b["id"], b["name"], b["input"])
                        for b in content if b["type"] == "tool_use"],
            stop_reason=r.stop_reason,
            content=content,
            usage={"input_tokens": r.usage.input_tokens,
                   "output_tokens": r.usage.output_tokens},
        )


# ============ TOOLS: the model's hands ============

class ToolError(Exception):
    """An expected failure. Its message goes back to the model, so write it for the model."""


class Workspace:
    """Every path goes through here, so the agent cannot leave its project folder."""

    def __init__(self, root):
        self.root = Path(root).resolve()

    def path(self, rel):
        p = (self.root / rel).resolve()
        if p != self.root and self.root not in p.parents:
            raise ToolError(f"'{rel}' is outside the project. Use a path inside it.")
        return p


@dataclass
class Tool:
    name: str
    description: str         # the model reads this, so write it for the model
    schema: dict             # the inputs, described as JSON Schema
    run: object              # the Python function the harness calls
    read_only: bool = False  # True if the tool never changes anything

    def spec(self):
        return {"name": self.name, "description": self.description, "input_schema": self.schema}


# Commands run without writing .pyc caches, so a check never runs stale code.
CLEAN_ENV = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1"}


def make_tools(ws):
    """Four tools. Every error message tells the model what to do next."""

    def list_files():
        files = [p.relative_to(ws.root).as_posix() for p in sorted(ws.root.rglob("*")) if p.is_file()]
        return "\n".join(f for f in files if "__pycache__" not in f) or "(no files)"

    def read_file(path, start_line=1):
        p = ws.path(path)
        if not p.is_file():
            raise ToolError(f"No file at '{path}'. Call list_files to see what exists.")
        lines = p.read_text().splitlines()
        page = lines[start_line - 1:start_line + 199]
        text = "\n".join(f"{n}| {line}" for n, line in enumerate(page, start_line))
        last = start_line + len(page) - 1
        if last < len(lines):
            text += f"\n[lines {start_line}-{last} of {len(lines)}. Call again with start_line={last + 1}.]"
        return text or "(empty file)"

    def edit_file(path, old_text, new_text):
        p = ws.path(path)
        if not p.is_file():
            raise ToolError(f"No file at '{path}'. Call list_files to see what exists.")
        text = p.read_text()
        count = text.count(old_text)
        if count == 0:
            raise ToolError("old_text was not found. Read the file again: spaces must match exactly.")
        if count > 1:
            raise ToolError(f"old_text appears {count} times. Include more lines so it is unique.")
        p.write_text(text.replace(old_text, new_text))
        return f"Edited {path}."

    def run_command(command):
        try:
            r = subprocess.run(command, shell=True, cwd=ws.root, capture_output=True,
                               text=True, timeout=60, env=CLEAN_ENV)
        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
            raise ToolError("Timed out after 60 seconds. Try a smaller command.")
        return f"exit code {r.returncode}\n{(r.stdout + r.stderr).strip() or '(no output)'}"

    string = {"type": "string"}
    tools = [
        Tool("list_files", "List every file in the project. Start here.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {}}, list_files, read_only=True),
        Tool("read_file", "Read a text file with line numbers, 200 lines per page.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"path": string, "start_line": {"type": "integer"}},
              "required": ["path"]}, read_file, read_only=True),
        Tool("edit_file", "Replace one exact, unique piece of text in a file. Read the file first.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"path": string, "old_text": string, "new_text": string},
              "required": ["path", "old_text", "new_text"]}, edit_file),
        Tool("run_command", "Run a shell command in the project folder, for example the tests.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"command": string}, "required": ["command"]},
             run_command),
    ]
    return {t.name: t for t in tools}


# ============ PERMISSIONS: allow, ask, or deny ============

BLOCKED = [r"\brm\s", r"\bsudo\b", r"\bgit\s+push\b", r"\bcurl\b", r"\bwget\b"]  # never
ALLOWED = [r"^python\s+test_\w+\.py\b", r"^python\s+-m\s+pytest\b"]            # always fine
CHAINING = re.compile(r"[;&|`$<>]")  # an allowed command must not smuggle in a second one


@dataclass
class Verdict:
    decision: str  # "allow", "ask", or "deny"
    reason: str    # the model reads this too, so say why


def decide(tool, args, mode):
    """The policy. Deny rules are checked before allow rules."""
    if tool.read_only:
        return Verdict("allow", "read-only tool")
    if tool.name == "run_command":
        command = args.get("command", "").strip()
        if any(re.search(p, command) for p in BLOCKED):
            return Verdict("deny", "destructive, network, or admin command")
        if any(re.search(p, command) for p in ALLOWED) and not CHAINING.search(command):
            return Verdict("allow", "allow-listed command")
        return Verdict("ask", "this command is not on the allow-list")
    if mode == "auto":
        return Verdict("allow", "edits are allowed in auto mode")
    return Verdict("ask", "this tool changes files")


def ask_human(call, reason):
    answer = input(f"         ? allow {call.name}? ({reason}) [y/N] ")
    return answer.strip().lower() == "y"


# ============ TRACE: a record of everything ============

class Tracer:
    """Writes one JSON object per event to a file (JSONL), and keeps them for a summary."""

    def __init__(self, path):
        self.path, self.start, self.events = path, time.time(), []
        Path(path).write_text("")  # a fresh trace file for every run

    def event(self, kind, **data):
        record = {"t": round(time.time() - self.start, 3), "kind": kind, **data}
        self.events.append(record)
        with open(self.path, "a") as f:
            f.write(json.dumps(record) + "\n")

    def summary(self):
        counts = {}
        for e in self.events:
            if e["kind"] not in ("run_start", "run_end"):
                counts[e["kind"]] = counts.get(e["kind"], 0) + 1
        tokens = sum(e.get("input_tokens", 0) + e.get("output_tokens", 0) for e in self.events)
        return ", ".join(f"{n} {kind}" for kind, n in counts.items()) + f", {tokens} tokens"


# ============ CONTEXT: keep the desk clear ============

def estimate_tokens(messages):
    """About 4 characters per token. Rough, but good enough to decide when to compact."""
    return len(json.dumps(messages)) // 4


def truncate(text, max_chars):
    """Keep the head and the tail. In logs, the error is usually near the end."""
    if len(text) <= max_chars:
        return text
    half = max_chars // 2
    return f"{text[:half]}\n[... {len(text) - max_chars} characters cut by the harness ...]\n{text[-half:]}"


def compact(messages, task, keep_last=4):
    """Swap old history for a short summary. Keep the newest messages word for word."""
    cut = len(messages) - keep_last
    while cut > 1 and messages[cut]["role"] != "assistant":  # never split a tool call from its result
        cut -= 1
    if cut <= 1:
        return None  # nothing old enough to summarize yet
    used = [b["name"] for m in messages[:cut] if isinstance(m["content"], list)
            for b in m["content"] if b.get("type") == "tool_use"]
    summary = ("[The harness summarized earlier work to save space.]\n"
               f"Original task: {task}\n"
               f"Tools used so far: {', '.join(used) or 'none'}\n"
               "Re-read any file before you edit it. Then continue the task.")
    return [{"role": "user", "content": summary}] + messages[cut:]


# ============ THE LOOP ============

SYSTEM_PROMPT = ("You are Rein, a careful coding agent working inside one project folder. "
                 "Look before you change anything, and run the tests to prove your work.")


@dataclass
class Config:
    max_turns: int = 10  # hard stop: the most model calls one run may use
    workspace: str = "demo"  # the only folder the agent may touch
    mode: str = "ask"        # "ask": edits need a human yes. "auto": edits are allowed
    trace_file: str = "trace.jsonl"  # every event of the run is recorded here
    tool_output_chars: int = 2000    # cap on each tool result that enters the context
    context_limit: int = 20000       # estimated tokens before old history is compacted


class Agent:
    def __init__(self, model, cfg, trace):
        self.model, self.cfg, self.trace = model, cfg, trace
        self.tools = make_tools(Workspace(cfg.workspace))
        self.specs = [t.spec() for t in self.tools.values()]
        self.denied = set()  # once denied, always denied

    def say(self, tag, text):
        show(tag, text)

    def finish(self, status, note, answer=""):
        """Every way a run can end goes through here, so none of them gets forgotten."""
        self.say(status, note)
        self.trace.event("run_end", status=status)
        return answer

    def run(self, task):
        messages = [{"role": "user", "content": task}]
        self.say("task", task)
        self.trace.event("run_start", task=task)
        for turn in range(1, self.cfg.max_turns + 1):
            size = estimate_tokens(messages)
            smaller = compact(messages, task) if size > self.cfg.context_limit else None
            if smaller:
                messages[:] = smaller  # change the list in place, so every holder sees it
                self.say("compact", f"~{size} tokens is over the limit ({self.cfg.context_limit}); "
                                    f"now ~{estimate_tokens(messages)}")
                self.trace.event("compact", before=size, after=estimate_tokens(messages))
            reply = self.model.call(SYSTEM_PROMPT, messages, self.specs)
            self.trace.event("model_call", turn=turn, stop_reason=reply.stop_reason, **reply.usage)
            messages.append({"role": "assistant", "content": reply.content})
            self.say(f"turn {turn}", f"model: {reply.text}" if reply.text else "model asks for tools")
            if not reply.tool_calls:
                return self.finish("done", f"after {turn} model calls", reply.text)
            results = []
            for call in reply.tool_calls:
                self.say("", f"tool   {call.name} {json.dumps(call.input)}")
                output, is_error = self.run_tool(call)
                self.trace.event("tool_call", tool=call.name, error=is_error, chars=len(output))
                self.say("", ("error  " if is_error else "result ") + one_line(output))
                results.append({"type": "tool_result", "tool_use_id": call.id,
                                "content": output, "is_error": is_error})
            messages.append({"role": "user", "content": results})
        return self.finish("stopped", f"turn limit reached ({self.cfg.max_turns} model calls)")

    def run_tool(self, call):
        """Run one tool call. Any error becomes a message for the model, never a crash."""
        tool = self.tools.get(call.name)
        if tool is None:
            return f"Unknown tool '{call.name}'. Available tools: {', '.join(self.tools)}.", True
        verdict = self.check_permission(tool, call)
        self.say("", f"{verdict.decision:<6} {verdict.reason}")
        if verdict.decision == "deny":
            return f"Permission denied: {verdict.reason}. Do not retry this; find another way.", True
        try:
            output = tool.run(**call.input)
        except ToolError as e:
            return str(e), True
        except Exception as e:  # a bug, or an argument the model made up
            return f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}", True
        if len(output) > self.cfg.tool_output_chars:
            self.say("", f"cut    {len(output)} characters down to {self.cfg.tool_output_chars}")
        return truncate(output, self.cfg.tool_output_chars), False

    def check_permission(self, tool, call):
        key = call.name + json.dumps(call.input, sort_keys=True)
        if key in self.denied:
            verdict = Verdict("deny", "this exact call was already denied")
        else:
            verdict = decide(tool, call.input, self.cfg.mode)
        if verdict.decision == "ask":
            yes = ask_human(call, verdict.reason)
            verdict = Verdict("allow", "a human said yes") if yes else Verdict("deny", "a human said no")
        if verdict.decision == "deny":
            self.denied.add(key)
        self.trace.event("permission", tool=call.name, decision=verdict.decision, reason=verdict.reason)
        return verdict


# ============ COMMAND LINE ============

def main():
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Rein: a small coding-agent harness")
    p.add_argument("task", help="what you want done, in plain words")
    p.add_argument("--fake", metavar="SCENARIO", help="use a scripted model from this JSON file")
    p.add_argument("--workspace", default="demo", help="the project folder the agent works in")
    p.add_argument("--mode", default="ask", choices=["ask", "auto"], help="auto: allow edits without asking")
    p.add_argument("--context-limit", type=int, default=20000, help="compact above this many tokens")
    args = p.parse_args()

    if args.fake:
        with open(args.fake) as f:
            scenario = json.load(f)
        model = FakeModel(scenario["replies"])
    else:
        model = ClaudeModel()

    cfg = Config(
        workspace=args.workspace,
        mode=args.mode,
        context_limit=args.context_limit,
    )
    trace = Tracer(cfg.trace_file)
    Agent(model, cfg, trace).run(args.task)
    show("trace", f"{trace.summary()} (details in {cfg.trace_file})")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Carry this forward

  • If it is not in the trace, you cannot debug it or measure it. Write events as they happen.
  • A generic summary shows new behavior without new code.
  • Cap every tool result, and keep both the head and the tail.
  • Compact old history, keep recent turns exact, and never separate a tool request from its result.

Steps 7 and 8: failing well, stopping well

Real runs go wrong in boring ways: the API is busy, the model repeats itself, a reply gets cut off, the money runs out, you press Ctrl+C. Rein learns to handle each one on purpose, instead of by crashing.

7 Surviving failures

GoalWhen the API fails for a moment, wait and try again. When it keeps failing, stop and say so clearly. TheoryChapter 15: errors are the main path Sizerein.py grows to 454 lines

7aTeach the models to fail

TransientError names a failure worth retrying: a busy server, a rate limit, a network blip. The FakeModel learns to pretend: a script step with "error" raises one, and "forever": true turns it into an outage that never ends.

The real model gets two changes. max_retries=0 switches off the SDK's own retries, and the SDK's retryable errors become TransientError. Why switch off retries that already work? Because you cannot see them. From now on Rein owns the one retry policy, and every retry is counted and traced.

7bRetries and a breaker

with_retries() calls the model and, after a Transient error, waits and tries again: up to 0.5 seconds, then 1, then 2, doubling each time but never above cap. The actual wait is a random amount between half and all of that. This Jitter means that a thousand clients that failed at the same moment do not all retry at the same moment. After the last attempt, it gives up and lets the caller decide.

The Circuit breaker counts failed calls in a row. After three, it is open: stop, and say clearly why.

rein.py+29 −0
new code
# ============ RECOVERY: counted, limited, able to give up ============def with_retries(call, on_retry, attempts=3, base=0.5, cap=8.0):    """Try again after a transient failure, waiting longer each time."""    for attempt in range(1, attempts + 1):        try:            return call()        except TransientError as e:            if attempt == attempts:                raise                                    # out of attempts: the caller decides            delay = min(cap, base * 2 ** (attempt - 1))  # 0.5s, 1s, 2s, 4s... never above cap            on_retry(attempt, delay, e)            time.sleep(delay * random.uniform(0.5, 1.0))  # jitter: never retry in lockstepclass CircuitBreaker:    """After `threshold` failed calls in a row, stop trying and say so clearly."""    def __init__(self, threshold=3):        self.threshold, self.failures = threshold, 0    def record(self, ok):        self.failures = 0 if ok else self.failures + 1    @property    def is_open(self):        return self.failures >= self.threshold# ============ THE LOOP ============

7cWire it in

A successful call resets the breaker. A call that fails after all its attempts moves the breaker one step, is printed and traced, and then either stops the run, if the breaker is open, or tries again on the next turn.

Checkpoint: step 7, an outage

This one takes a few seconds. That is the backoff, waiting.
