What just happened

Look at turn 2. Instead of fixing add(), the agent made it return 5 for exactly the inputs the test uses, and subtract for everything else. The test passed, so the check passed too. A computational check can only see what it tests.

Then the reviewer started with a fresh context, read calc.py, and failed the change. The harness sent that verdict back, exactly like a failed check. The agent fixed add() properly, the check passed again, and a brand-new reviewer looked again. It tried to run the tests, was denied because it is read-only, and passed the change by reading it. Only then was the run done: 11 model calls instead of 6. That is the price of a second opinion.

What the agent did in turn 2 is called Reward hacking: satisfying the measurement instead of the goal. It is one of the failure modes AI labs worry about most, and it is why tests are necessary but not enough. Here the defense was an independent reviewer. Another defense is a better check: a test with more inputs would have caught it too.

Be honest about the limits. A reviewer is itself a model, an Inferential check, so it can be wrong in both directions: it can pass a bad change and fail a good one. It costs extra calls and time. Use it where the stakes justify it, and measure whether it actually helps, with the evals in B7.

Talking point: "My harness caught a reward hack that passed the tests. An independent reviewer sub-agent, with fresh context and read-only tools, reviewed the change, and its verdict went back through the same verification gate as a failing test."

Break it on purpose

  1. Run the checkpoint without --review. The hack is accepted as done. Run python demo/test_calc.py: it passes. Now open demo/calc.py and look at what "passing" means here.
  2. In review(), change mode="read_only" to mode="auto". The reviewer's test command now runs. Why is read-only still the right default for a reviewer? Because a reviewer that can change what it reviews is no longer independent.

Why does the reviewer start with a fresh context, instead of the main agent's history?

  • Only to save tokens.
  • So it judges the result with fresh eyes, without inheriting the main agent's assumptions and excuses.
  • Because a sub-agent cannot read a long history.

The main agent's history is full of its own reasoning, including the reasoning that led to the hack. A reviewer that reads it tends to agree with it. Isolation is what makes the second opinion independent.

The whole rein.py after step 11 (570 lines)
"""Rein: a small coding-agent harness, built one step at a time.

Each section below is one system of the harness. Read it from top to bottom.
"""
import argparse
import json
import os
import random
import re
import subprocess
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path


def show(tag, text, indent=""):
    """Print one line of the live transcript: line breaks become spaces, and it is cut to fit."""
    print(f"{indent}{tag:<8} {' '.join(str(text).splitlines())}"[:100])


def one_line(text):
    """Join the lines of a tool result with ' | ' for the transcript. The model still gets all of it."""
    return " | ".join(line.strip() for line in str(text).splitlines() if line.strip())


# ============ MODEL: one interface, two models ============

class TransientError(Exception):
    """A failure worth retrying: overloaded, rate-limited, or a network blip."""


@dataclass
class ToolCall:
    id: str      # every request gets an id, so its result can be matched to it
    name: str    # which tool the model wants
    input: dict  # the arguments the model chose


@dataclass
class Reply:
    text: str          # what the model wrote
    tool_calls: list   # the tools it asked for (maybe none)
    stop_reason: str   # why it stopped: "end_turn", "tool_use", "max_tokens", ...
    content: list      # the raw blocks, saved into the history exactly as they came
    usage: dict = field(default_factory=dict)  # input and output token counts


class FakeModel:
    """Replays a script of replies, so every run is free, offline, and repeatable."""

    def __init__(self, replies):
        self.replies = list(replies)
        self.calls = 0

    def call(self, system, messages, tools):
        step = self.replies.pop(0) if self.replies else {"say": "(the script has run out)"}
        if "error" in step:            # a script can pretend the API failed
            if step.get("forever"):     # an outage that never ends
                self.replies.insert(0, step)
            raise TransientError(step["error"])
        content, tool_calls = [], []
        if step.get("say"):
            content.append({"type": "text", "text": step["say"]})
        for name, args in step.get("tools", []):
            self.calls += 1
            call = ToolCall(f"call_{self.calls}", name, args)
            tool_calls.append(call)
            content.append({"type": "tool_use", "id": call.id, "name": name, "input": args})
        stop = step.get("stop") or ("tool_use" if tool_calls else "end_turn")
        usage = {"input_tokens": len(json.dumps(messages)) // 4,
                 "output_tokens": len(json.dumps(content)) // 4}
        return Reply(step.get("say", ""), tool_calls, stop, content, usage)


class ClaudeModel:
    """The real model, behind exactly the same interface."""

    def __init__(self, model=None):
        import anthropic
        self.client = anthropic.Anthropic(max_retries=0)  # the harness owns retries now
        self.model = model or os.environ.get("REIN_MODEL", "claude-sonnet-5")
        names = ("RateLimitError", "APIConnectionError", "InternalServerError", "OverloadedError")
        self.retryable = tuple(getattr(anthropic, n) for n in names if hasattr(anthropic, n))

    def call(self, system, messages, tools):
        extra = {"tools": tools} if tools else {}
        try:
            r = self.client.messages.create(model=self.model, max_tokens=4096,
                                            system=system, messages=messages, **extra)
        except self.retryable as e:
            raise TransientError(type(e).__name__) from e
        content = [block.model_dump(exclude_none=True) for block in r.content]
        return Reply(
            text="".join(b["text"] for b in content if b["type"] == "text"),
            tool_calls=[ToolCall(b["id"], b["name"], b["input"])
                        for b in content if b["type"] == "tool_use"],
            stop_reason=r.stop_reason,
            content=content,
            usage={"input_tokens": r.usage.input_tokens,
                   "output_tokens": r.usage.output_tokens},
        )


# ============ TOOLS: the model's hands ============

class ToolError(Exception):
    """An expected failure. Its message goes back to the model, so write it for the model."""


class Workspace:
    """Every path goes through here, so the agent cannot leave its project folder."""

    def __init__(self, root):
        self.root = Path(root).resolve()

    def path(self, rel):
        p = (self.root / rel).resolve()
        if p != self.root and self.root not in p.parents:
            raise ToolError(f"'{rel}' is outside the project. Use a path inside it.")
        return p


@dataclass
class Tool:
    name: str
    description: str         # the model reads this, so write it for the model
    schema: dict             # the inputs, described as JSON Schema
    run: object              # the Python function the harness calls
    read_only: bool = False  # True if the tool never changes anything

    def spec(self):
        return {"name": self.name, "description": self.description, "input_schema": self.schema}


# Commands run without writing .pyc caches, so a check never runs stale code.
CLEAN_ENV = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1"}


def make_tools(ws):
    """Four tools. Every error message tells the model what to do next."""

    def list_files():
        files = [p.relative_to(ws.root).as_posix() for p in sorted(ws.root.rglob("*")) if p.is_file()]
        return "\n".join(f for f in files if "__pycache__" not in f) or "(no files)"

    def read_file(path, start_line=1):
        p = ws.path(path)
        if not p.is_file():
            raise ToolError(f"No file at '{path}'. Call list_files to see what exists.")
        lines = p.read_text().splitlines()
        page = lines[start_line - 1:start_line + 199]
        text = "\n".join(f"{n}| {line}" for n, line in enumerate(page, start_line))
        last = start_line + len(page) - 1
        if last < len(lines):
            text += f"\n[lines {start_line}-{last} of {len(lines)}. Call again with start_line={last + 1}.]"
        return text or "(empty file)"

    def edit_file(path, old_text, new_text):
        p = ws.path(path)
        if not p.is_file():
            raise ToolError(f"No file at '{path}'. Call list_files to see what exists.")
        text = p.read_text()
        count = text.count(old_text)
        if count == 0:
            raise ToolError("old_text was not found. Read the file again: spaces must match exactly.")
        if count > 1:
            raise ToolError(f"old_text appears {count} times. Include more lines so it is unique.")
        p.write_text(text.replace(old_text, new_text))
        return f"Edited {path}."

    def run_command(command):
        try:
            r = subprocess.run(command, shell=True, cwd=ws.root, capture_output=True,
                               text=True, timeout=60, env=CLEAN_ENV)
        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
            raise ToolError("Timed out after 60 seconds. Try a smaller command.")
        return f"exit code {r.returncode}\n{(r.stdout + r.stderr).strip() or '(no output)'}"

    string = {"type": "string"}
    tools = [
        Tool("list_files", "List every file in the project. Start here.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {}}, list_files, read_only=True),
        Tool("read_file", "Read a text file with line numbers, 200 lines per page.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"path": string, "start_line": {"type": "integer"}},
              "required": ["path"]}, read_file, read_only=True),
        Tool("edit_file", "Replace one exact, unique piece of text in a file. Read the file first.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"path": string, "old_text": string, "new_text": string},
              "required": ["path", "old_text", "new_text"]}, edit_file),
        Tool("run_command", "Run a shell command in the project folder, for example the tests.",
             {"type": "object", "properties": {"command": string}, "required": ["command"]},
             run_command),
    ]
    return {t.name: t for t in tools}


# ============ PERMISSIONS: allow, ask, or deny ============

BLOCKED = [r"\brm\s", r"\bsudo\b", r"\bgit\s+push\b", r"\bcurl\b", r"\bwget\b"]  # never
ALLOWED = [r"^python\s+test_\w+\.py\b", r"^python\s+-m\s+pytest\b"]            # always fine
CHAINING = re.compile(r"[;&|`$<>]")  # an allowed command must not smuggle in a second one


@dataclass
class Verdict:
    decision: str  # "allow", "ask", or "deny"
    reason: str    # the model reads this too, so say why


def decide(tool, args, mode):
    """The policy. Deny rules are checked before allow rules."""
    if tool.read_only:
        return Verdict("allow", "read-only tool")
    if mode == "read_only":
        return Verdict("deny", "this agent is read-only")
    if tool.name == "run_command":
        command = args.get("command", "").strip()
        if any(re.search(p, command) for p in BLOCKED):
            return Verdict("deny", "destructive, network, or admin command")
        if any(re.search(p, command) for p in ALLOWED) and not CHAINING.search(command):
            return Verdict("allow", "allow-listed command")
        return Verdict("ask", "this command is not on the allow-list")
    if mode == "auto":
        return Verdict("allow", "edits are allowed in auto mode")
    return Verdict("ask", "this tool changes files")


def ask_human(call, reason):
    answer = input(f"         ? allow {call.name}? ({reason}) [y/N] ")
    return answer.strip().lower() == "y"


# ============ TRACE: a record of everything ============

class Tracer:
    """Writes one JSON object per event to a file (JSONL), and keeps them for a summary."""

    def __init__(self, path):
        self.path, self.start, self.events = path, time.time(), []
        Path(path).write_text("")  # a fresh trace file for every run

    def event(self, kind, **data):
        record = {"t": round(time.time() - self.start, 3), "kind": kind, **data}
        self.events.append(record)
        with open(self.path, "a") as f:
            f.write(json.dumps(record) + "\n")

    def summary(self):
        counts = {}
        for e in self.events:
            if e["kind"] not in ("run_start", "run_end"):
                counts[e["kind"]] = counts.get(e["kind"], 0) + 1
        tokens = sum(e.get("input_tokens", 0) + e.get("output_tokens", 0) for e in self.events)
        return ", ".join(f"{n} {kind}" for kind, n in counts.items()) + f", {tokens} tokens"


# ============ CONTEXT: keep the desk clear ============

def estimate_tokens(messages):
    """About 4 characters per token. Rough, but good enough to decide when to compact."""
    return len(json.dumps(messages)) // 4


def truncate(text, max_chars):
    """Keep the head and the tail. In logs, the error is usually near the end."""
    if len(text) <= max_chars:
        return text
    half = max_chars // 2
    return f"{text[:half]}\n[... {len(text) - max_chars} characters cut by the harness ...]\n{text[-half:]}"


def compact(messages, task, keep_last=4):
    """Swap old history for a short summary. Keep the newest messages word for word."""
    cut = len(messages) - keep_last
    while cut > 1 and messages[cut]["role"] != "assistant":  # never split a tool call from its result
        cut -= 1
    if cut <= 1:
        return None  # nothing old enough to summarize yet
    used = [b["name"] for m in messages[:cut] if isinstance(m["content"], list)
            for b in m["content"] if b.get("type") == "tool_use"]
    summary = ("[The harness summarized earlier work to save space.]\n"
               f"Original task: {task}\n"
               f"Tools used so far: {', '.join(used) or 'none'}\n"
               "Re-read any file before you edit it. Then continue the task.")
    return [{"role": "user", "content": summary}] + messages[cut:]


# ============ RECOVERY: counted, limited, able to give up ============

def with_retries(call, on_retry, attempts=3, base=0.5, cap=8.0):
    """Try again after a transient failure, waiting longer each time."""
    for attempt in range(1, attempts + 1):
        try:
            return call()
        except TransientError as e:
            if attempt == attempts:
                raise                                    # out of attempts: the caller decides
            delay = min(cap, base * 2 ** (attempt - 1))  # 0.5s, 1s, 2s, 4s... never above cap
            on_retry(attempt, delay, e)
            time.sleep(delay * random.uniform(0.5, 1.0))  # jitter: never retry in lockstep


class CircuitBreaker:
    """After `threshold` failed calls in a row, stop trying and say so clearly."""

    def __init__(self, threshold=3):
        self.threshold, self.failures = threshold, 0

    def record(self, ok):
        self.failures = 0 if ok else self.failures + 1

    @property
    def is_open(self):
        return self.failures >= self.threshold


class LoopDetector:
    """Notices the exact same tool call, made several times in a row."""

    def __init__(self, limit=3):
        self.limit, self.last, self.count = limit, None, 0

    def repeated(self, call):
        key = call.name + json.dumps(call.input, sort_keys=True)
        self.count = self.count + 1 if key == self.last else 1
        self.last = key
        return self.count >= self.limit


def close_open_calls(messages):
    """After an interrupt, every tool request still needs a result, or the history is broken."""
    last = messages[-1]
    if last["role"] == "assistant":
        ids = [b["id"] for b in last["content"] if b.get("type") == "tool_use"]
        if ids:
            messages.append({"role": "user", "content": [
                {"type": "tool_result", "tool_use_id": i, "content": "Interrupted before it ran.",
                 "is_error": True} for i in ids]})


# ============ THE LOOP ============

BASE_PROMPT = """You are Rein, a careful coding agent working inside one project folder.
Rules, most important first:
1. Stay inside the project folder. Use relative paths.
2. Look before you change anything. Make the smallest change that solves the task.
3. Never say something works unless you ran a check that proves it.
4. If a tool call is denied, do not repeat it. Find another way, or explain what you need."""


def build_system_prompt(workspace):
    """Build the prompt from layers. When two layers disagree, the earlier one wins."""
    layers = [BASE_PROMPT]
    rules = Path(workspace) / "AGENTS.md"
    if rules.is_file():
        layers.append("Project rules from AGENTS.md (follow them unless they break a rule above):\n"
                      + rules.read_text())
    return "\n\n".join(layers)


REVIEWER_PROMPT = """You are an independent reviewer. You did NOT write this change.
Read the files and decide whether the task is truly solved for every input, not only for the tests.
You cannot change anything. Start your answer with exactly VERDICT: PASS or VERDICT: FAIL."""

@dataclass
class Config:
    max_turns: int = 10  # hard stop: the most model calls one run may use
    workspace: str = "demo"  # the only folder the agent may touch
    mode: str = "ask"        # "ask": edits need a human yes. "auto": edits are allowed
    trace_file: str = "trace.jsonl"  # every event of the run is recorded here
    tool_output_chars: int = 2000    # cap on each tool result that enters the context
    context_limit: int = 20000       # estimated tokens before old history is compacted
    token_budget: int = 200000       # hard stop: the most tokens one run may spend
    check: str = ""                  # a command that proves the work, like "python test_calc.py"
    max_fix_rounds: int = 2          # how many times the gate may send work back
    review: bool = False             # also ask an independent reviewer sub-agent


class Agent:
    def __init__(self, model, cfg, trace, name="main", system=None, reviewer_model=None):
        self.model, self.cfg, self.trace = model, cfg, trace
        self.name = name
        self.reviewer_model = reviewer_model or model
        self.tools = make_tools(Workspace(cfg.workspace))
        self.specs = [t.spec() for t in self.tools.values()]
        self.system = system or build_system_prompt(cfg.workspace)  # built once: stable, so it caches well
        self.denied = set()  # once denied, always denied
        self.breaker = CircuitBreaker()
        self.loops = LoopDetector()

    def say(self, tag, text):
        show(tag, text, "" if self.name == "main" else "    ")

    def finish(self, status, note, answer=""):
        """Every way a run can end goes through here, so none of them gets forgotten."""
        self.say(status, note)
        self.trace.event("run_end", status=status)
        return answer

    def on_retry(self, attempt, delay, error):
        self.say("retry", f"attempt {attempt} failed ({error}); waiting up to {delay:.1f}s")
        self.trace.event("retry", attempt=attempt, error=str(error))

    def run(self, task):
        messages = [{"role": "user", "content": task}]
        try:
            return self.loop(task, messages)
        except KeyboardInterrupt:  # Ctrl+C: stop cleanly and leave a valid history behind
            close_open_calls(messages)
            return self.finish("stopped", f"interrupted; history left valid ({len(messages)} messages)")

    def loop(self, task, messages):
        self.say("task", task)
        self.trace.event("run_start", task=task)
        tokens = fix_rounds = 0
        for turn in range(1, self.cfg.max_turns + 1):
            if tokens >= self.cfg.token_budget:
                return self.finish("stopped", f"token budget spent ({tokens} of {self.cfg.token_budget})")
            size = estimate_tokens(messages)
            smaller = compact(messages, task) if size > self.cfg.context_limit else None
            if smaller:
                messages[:] = smaller  # change the list in place, so every holder sees it
                self.say("compact", f"~{size} tokens is over the limit ({self.cfg.context_limit}); "
                                    f"now ~{estimate_tokens(messages)}")
                self.trace.event("compact", before=size, after=estimate_tokens(messages))
            try:
                reply = with_retries(lambda: self.model.call(self.system, messages, self.specs),
                                     on_retry=self.on_retry)
                self.breaker.record(ok=True)
            except TransientError as e:
                self.breaker.record(ok=False)
                self.say("error", f"model call failed ({e}); breaker at "
                                  f"{self.breaker.failures} of {self.breaker.threshold}")
                self.trace.event("model_error", error=str(e))
                if self.breaker.is_open:
                    return self.finish("stopped", "the model API keeps failing; the circuit breaker is open")
                continue
            tokens += reply.usage.get("input_tokens", 0) + reply.usage.get("output_tokens", 0)
            self.trace.event("model_call", turn=turn, stop_reason=reply.stop_reason, **reply.usage)
            messages.append({"role": "assistant", "content": reply.content})
            self.say(f"turn {turn}", f"model: {reply.text}" if reply.text else "model asks for tools")
            if reply.stop_reason == "max_tokens" and not reply.tool_calls:
                self.say("cut", "the reply was cut off; asking the model to continue")
                messages.append({"role": "user", "content": "You were cut off. Continue exactly where you stopped."})
                continue
            if not reply.tool_calls:  # the model says it is done. That is a claim: check it.
                passed, report = self.verify(task, reply.text)
                if passed:
                    return self.finish("done", f"after {turn} model calls", reply.text)
                if fix_rounds == self.cfg.max_fix_rounds:
                    return self.finish("unverified", f"still failing after {fix_rounds} fix rounds", reply.text)
                fix_rounds += 1
                self.say("check", f"not done: {one_line(report)}")
                messages.append({"role": "user", "content": "The harness checked your work and it is not done "
                                 f"yet:\n{report}\nFix the problem, and check it yourself before you finish."})
                continue
            results = []
            for call in reply.tool_calls:
                self.say("", f"tool   {call.name} {json.dumps(call.input)}")
                output, is_error = self.run_tool(call)
                self.trace.event("tool_call", tool=call.name, error=is_error, chars=len(output))
                self.say("", ("error  " if is_error else "result ") + one_line(output))
                results.append({"type": "tool_result", "tool_use_id": call.id,
                                "content": output, "is_error": is_error})
            messages.append({"role": "user", "content": results})
        return self.finish("stopped", f"turn limit reached ({self.cfg.max_turns} model calls)")

    def run_tool(self, call):
        """Run one tool call. Any error becomes a message for the model, never a crash."""
        tool = self.tools.get(call.name)
        if tool is None:
            return f"Unknown tool '{call.name}'. Available tools: {', '.join(self.tools)}.", True
        if self.loops.repeated(call):
            return "You made this exact call 3 times in a row. It is not helping. Try something else.", True
        verdict = self.check_permission(tool, call)
        self.say("", f"{verdict.decision:<6} {verdict.reason}")
        if verdict.decision == "deny":
            return f"Permission denied: {verdict.reason}. Do not retry this; find another way.", True
        try:
            output = tool.run(**call.input)
        except ToolError as e:
            return str(e), True
        except Exception as e:  # a bug, or an argument the model made up
            return f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}", True
        if len(output) > self.cfg.tool_output_chars:
            self.say("", f"cut    {len(output)} characters down to {self.cfg.tool_output_chars}")
        return truncate(output, self.cfg.tool_output_chars), False

    def verify(self, task, claim):
        """'Done' is a claim. Run the check command and see for ourselves."""
        if self.cfg.check:
            r = subprocess.run(self.cfg.check, shell=True, cwd=self.cfg.workspace, capture_output=True,
                               text=True, timeout=300, env=CLEAN_ENV)
            self.trace.event("verify", check=self.cfg.check, passed=r.returncode == 0)
            if r.returncode != 0:
                return False, (r.stdout + r.stderr).strip()
            self.say("check", f"passed: {self.cfg.check}")
        if self.cfg.review:
            return self.review(task, claim)
        return True, "ok"

    def review(self, task, claim):
        """A sub-agent: fresh history, read-only powers, its own limits. Only its verdict comes back."""
        self.say("review", "starting an independent reviewer (fresh context, read-only)")
        cfg = Config(workspace=self.cfg.workspace, mode="read_only", max_turns=6)
        reviewer = Agent(self.reviewer_model, cfg, self.trace, name="reviewer", system=REVIEWER_PROMPT)
        verdict = reviewer.run(f"The task was: {task}\nThe agent says: {claim}\nIs the task truly done?")
        passed = verdict.strip().upper().startswith("VERDICT: PASS")
        self.trace.event("verify", check="review", passed=passed)
        self.say("review", f"verdict: {'PASS' if passed else 'FAIL'}")
        return passed, verdict

    def check_permission(self, tool, call):
        key = call.name + json.dumps(call.input, sort_keys=True)
        if key in self.denied:
            verdict = Verdict("deny", "this exact call was already denied")
        else:
            verdict = decide(tool, call.input, self.cfg.mode)
        if verdict.decision == "ask":
            yes = ask_human(call, verdict.reason)
            verdict = Verdict("allow", "a human said yes") if yes else Verdict("deny", "a human said no")
        if verdict.decision == "deny":
            self.denied.add(key)
        self.trace.event("permission", tool=call.name, decision=verdict.decision, reason=verdict.reason)
        return verdict


# ============ COMMAND LINE ============

def main():
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Rein: a small coding-agent harness")
    p.add_argument("task", nargs="?", help="what you want done, in plain words")
    p.add_argument("--fake", metavar="SCENARIO", help="use a scripted model from this JSON file")
    p.add_argument("--workspace", default="demo", help="the project folder the agent works in")
    p.add_argument("--mode", default="ask", choices=["ask", "auto"], help="auto: allow edits without asking")
    p.add_argument("--context-limit", type=int, default=20000, help="compact above this many tokens")
    p.add_argument("--budget", type=int, default=200000, help="stop after spending this many tokens")
    p.add_argument("--check", default="", help='a command that proves the work, e.g. "python test_calc.py"')
    p.add_argument("--show-prompt", action="store_true", help="print the system prompt and stop")
    p.add_argument("--review", action="store_true", help="also ask an independent reviewer sub-agent")
    args = p.parse_args()
    if args.show_prompt:
        print(build_system_prompt(args.workspace))
        return
    if not args.task:
        p.error("tell Rein what to do, in quotes")

    reviewer_model = None
    if args.fake:
        with open(args.fake) as f:
            scenario = json.load(f)
        model = FakeModel(scenario["replies"])
        reviewer_model = FakeModel(scenario.get("reviewer", []))  # the reviewer has its own script
    else:
        model = ClaudeModel()

    cfg = Config(
        workspace=args.workspace,
        mode=args.mode,
        context_limit=args.context_limit,
        token_budget=args.budget,
        check=args.check,
        review=args.review,
    )
    trace = Tracer(cfg.trace_file)
    Agent(model, cfg, trace, reviewer_model=reviewer_model).run(args.task)
    show("trace", f"{trace.summary()} (details in {cfg.trace_file})")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

What you built

One file, 570 lines, with every wire from Part 3 in code you wrote and tested yourself:

WireWhere it lives in rein.pyStep
Control planeBASE_PROMPT, build_system_prompt(), AGENTS.md10
LoopAgent.loop(), finish(), the guards2, 8
ToolsWorkspace, make_tools()2, 3
Permissionsdecide(), check_permission()4, 11
Contexttruncate(), compact()6
Recoverywith_retries(), CircuitBreaker, LoopDetector7, 8
Verificationverify(), review()9, 11
ObservabilityTracer5

Read the whole file once more, top to bottom. You should be able to explain every line, and say which failure it prevents. If a line feels fuzzy, go back to its step and break it on purpose. That fluency is exactly what interviewers probe for.

Carry this forward

  • Tests can be gamed. An independent reviewer judges the result itself.
  • A sub-agent is the same loop, with a fresh history, fewer powers, and its own limits.
  • Only the verdict crosses back. The contexts stay separate.
  • A second opinion costs calls, so measure whether it pays.

From one file to a project

One file was the right shape for learning: one place to look, every change visible. A project you show needs more: automated tests, measured evals, and a clear write-up. This chapter takes you there.

Your checkpoints, automated

Every checkpoint was a test you ran by hand: a script, a run, and a look at what happened. A computer can do the looking. Save this next to rein.py as test_rein.py:

"""Your checkpoints, automated. Put this file next to rein.py and run:  python -m pytest -q"""
import sys

import rein
from rein import Agent, Config, FakeModel, Tracer

BUGGY = "def add(a, b):\n    return a - b\n"
TEST = "from calc import add\n\nif add(2, 3) != 5:\n    raise SystemExit('add(2, 3) is wrong')\n"


def run(tmp_path, replies, **settings):
    """Run Rein with a scripted model on a fresh copy of the demo. Return the trace events."""
    (tmp_path / "calc.py").write_text(BUGGY)
    (tmp_path / "test_calc.py").write_text(TEST)
    cfg = Config(workspace=str(tmp_path), mode="auto", trace_file=str(tmp_path / "trace.jsonl"), **settings)
    trace = Tracer(cfg.trace_file)
    Agent(FakeModel(replies), cfg, trace).run("Fix the failing test in calc.py")
    return trace.events


def find(events, kind, **match):
    return [e for e in events if e["kind"] == kind and all(e.get(k) == v for k, v in match.items())]


def test_the_wall_blocks_paths_outside_the_project(tmp_path):
    events = run(tmp_path, [{"tools": [["read_file", {"path": "../secret.txt"}]]}, {"say": "Done."}])
    assert find(events, "tool_call", tool="read_file", error=True)


def test_the_gate_denies_destructive_commands(tmp_path):
    events = run(tmp_path, [{"tools": [["run_command", {"command": "rm -rf ."}]]}, {"say": "Done."}])
    assert find(events, "permission", decision="deny")
    assert (tmp_path / "calc.py").exists()


def test_the_verification_gate_sends_a_wrong_fix_back(tmp_path):
    wrong = {"tools": [["edit_file", {"path": "calc.py", "old_text": "a - b", "new_text": "a * b"}]]}
    right = {"tools": [["edit_file", {"path": "calc.py", "old_text": "a * b", "new_text": "a + b"}]]}
    events = run(tmp_path, [wrong, {"say": "Done!"}, right, {"say": "Done, for real."}],
                 check=f'"{sys.executable}" test_calc.py')
    assert [e["passed"] for e in find(events, "verify")] == [False, True]
    assert "a + b" in (tmp_path / "calc.py").read_text()


def test_the_breaker_opens_during_an_outage(tmp_path, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(rein.time, "sleep", lambda seconds: None)  # tests should not really wait
    events = run(tmp_path, [{"error": "overloaded", "forever": True}])
    assert len(find(events, "model_error")) == 3
    assert find(events, "run_end", status="stopped")
pip install pytest
python -m pytest -q test_rein.py

The last line should say 4 passed, followed by how long it took. Name the file in the command: otherwise pytest also finds demo/test_calc.py, and its result depends on whether the demo is fixed right now.

Three ideas make these tests work. First, pytest gives each test its own empty folder, tmp_path, so every test builds a fresh demo and nothing leaks between tests. Second, the tests read the trace instead of the screen. The trace you built in step 5 is what makes the harness testable: events are data, and data is easy to check. Third, monkeypatch swaps out time.sleep for one test, so the outage test does not really wait.

Now write one yourself: turn the step 8 checkpoint into a test that checks the loop detector fired. Hint: the tool result is an error, so look for a tool_call event with error set to true on the third read.

The reference project

The download with this book includes Rein grown into a small Python package, with 20 tests and an eval runner. It is the same design, reorganized: each section of your file became a module. Here is where everything went:

Section of rein.pyReference fileWhat changed
MODELrein/model.pyClaudeModel is called AnthropicModel. The fake model records every request, so tests can check exactly what was sent.
TOOLSrein/tools.pySix tools: adds search and write_file.
PERMISSIONSrein/permissions.pyA Policy class with three modes: ask, auto_edit, and read_only.
TRACErein/trace.pySafe to use from several threads, because read-only tools can run side by side.
CONTEXTrein/context.pyThe compaction summary also lists files changed, recent commands, and recent errors.
RECOVERYrein/recovery.pyThe same three pieces. The sleep function can be swapped out in tests.
THE LOOPrein/loop.pyReturns a result with a status (done, unverified, max_turns, budget, circuit_open, interrupted). Runs read-only tools in parallel. Handles a reply cut off in the middle of a tool call. Tags every event with the agent's name.
COMMAND LINErein/__main__.pyRun it with python -m rein.

Unzip it, and with your build's virtual environment still active:

cd reference
pip install pytest
python -m pytest -q          # 20 offline tests, no API key needed
python -m rein "The tests fail. Fix the bug." --workspace examples/demo \
    --mode auto_edit --check "python -m pytest -q" --fake examples/fix_add_script.json

On Windows, type the last command on one line, without the backslash. It really edits examples/demo. Copy the folder first if you want to run it more than once.

Read the reference test file (20 tests)
"""Harness tests. No API key, no network, no randomness in outcomes: FakeModel plays the model.

This is the most important habit in the whole project: the harness is ordinary
software, so we test it like ordinary software.
"""
import json

import pytest

from rein import Agent, Config, FakeModel, Policy, Tracer
from rein.context import compact
from rein.recovery import with_retries


@pytest.fixture
def repo(tmp_path):
    (tmp_path / "calc.py").write_text("def add(a, b):\n    return a - b\n")
    (tmp_path / "test_calc.py").write_text("from calc import add\n\ndef test_add():\n    assert add(2, 3) == 5\n")
    return tmp_path


def agent(script, repo, mode="auto_edit", **cfg):
    return Agent(FakeModel(script), repo, Policy(mode, ask=lambda *_: False), Config(**cfg), Tracer())


def test_happy_path_edit_and_finish(repo):
    a = agent([
        {"tool": "read_file", "input": {"path": "calc.py"}},
        {"tool": "edit_file", "input": {"path": "calc.py", "old_text": "a - b", "new_text": "a + b"}},
        {"say": "Fixed add()."},
    ], repo)
    r = a.run("fix the bug")
    assert r.status == "done" and r.turns == 3
    assert "a + b" in (repo / "calc.py").read_text()


def test_every_tool_use_gets_a_matching_result(repo):
    a = agent([{"tools": [["read_file", {"path": "calc.py"}], ["list_files", {}]]}, {"say": "ok"}], repo)
    r = a.run("look around")
    uses = [b["id"] for m in r.messages if m["role"] == "assistant" for b in m["content"] if b["type"] == "tool_use"]
    results = [b["tool_use_id"] for m in r.messages if m["role"] == "user" and isinstance(m["content"], list)
               for b in m["content"] if b["type"] == "tool_result"]
    assert uses == results


def test_dangerous_command_is_denied_and_sticky(repo):
    rm = {"command": "rm -rf ."}
    a = agent([{"tool": "run_command", "input": rm}, {"tool": "run_command", "input": rm}, {"say": "blocked"}], repo)
    r = a.run("clean up")
    denials = [b for m in r.messages if isinstance(m["content"], list) for b in m["content"]
               if b.get("type") == "tool_result" and "Permission denied" in b["content"]]
    assert len(denials) == 2 and (repo / "calc.py").exists()
    assert r.stats["denied"] == 2


def test_allow_list_cannot_be_smuggled(repo):
    tool = Agent(FakeModel([]), repo).tools["run_command"]
    policy = Policy("auto_edit", ask=lambda *_: False)
    assert policy.decide(tool, {"command": "python -m pytest -q"}).verdict == "allow"
    assert policy.decide(tool, {"command": "pytest && python leak.py"}).verdict == "ask"
    assert policy.decide(tool, {"command": "pytest; cat ~/.ssh/id_rsa"}).verdict == "deny"


def test_read_only_mode_blocks_edits(repo):
    a = agent([{"tool": "write_file", "input": {"path": "x.py", "content": "boom"}}, {"say": "done"}], repo, mode="read_only")
    a.run("write something")
    assert not (repo / "x.py").exists()


def test_unknown_tool_and_missing_args_are_repaired_not_crashed(repo):
    a = agent([{"tool": "delete_everything", "input": {}}, {"tool": "read_file", "input": {}}, {"say": "ok"}], repo)
    r = a.run("x")
    texts = [b["content"] for m in r.messages if isinstance(m["content"], list) for b in m["content"]
             if b.get("type") == "tool_result"]
    assert "Unknown tool" in texts[0] and "Missing required" in texts[1] and r.status == "done"


def test_workspace_escape_is_refused(repo):
    a = agent([{"tool": "read_file", "input": {"path": "../../etc/passwd"}}, {"say": "ok"}], repo)
    r = a.run("x")
    result = r.messages[2]["content"][0]
    assert result["is_error"] and "outside the project" in result["content"]


def test_transient_errors_are_retried(repo, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr("time.sleep", lambda s: None)
    a = agent([{"error": "overloaded"}, {"error": "overloaded"}, {"say": "done"}], repo)
    r = a.run("x")
    assert r.status == "done" and r.stats["retries"] == 2


def test_circuit_breaker_opens(repo, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr("time.sleep", lambda s: None)
    a = agent([{"error": "down"}] * 20, repo)
    r = a.run("x")
    assert r.status == "circuit_open"


def test_backoff_grows_and_is_capped():
    from rein.model import TransientModelError
    delays = []

    def flaky():
        raise TransientModelError("down")
    with pytest.raises(TransientModelError):
        with_retries(flaky, attempts=5, base=1, cap=4, sleep=delays.append)
    assert len(delays) == 4 and max(delays) <= 4 and delays[1] > delays[0] * 0.5


def test_loop_detector_breaks_repetition(repo):
    same = {"tool": "read_file", "input": {"path": "calc.py"}}
    a = agent([same, same, same, {"say": "ok"}], repo)
    r = a.run("x")
    third = r.messages[6]["content"][0]["content"]
    assert "3 times in a row" in third


def test_verification_gate_sends_work_back(repo):
    a = agent([
        {"say": "All done!"},  # a false claim: the test still fails
        {"tool": "edit_file", "input": {"path": "calc.py", "old_text": "a - b", "new_text": "a + b"}},
        {"say": "Now it is really fixed."},
    ], repo, check_command="python -m pytest -q")
    r = a.run("fix it")
    assert r.status == "done" and r.turns == 3
    assert "NOT done" in r.messages[2]["content"]


def test_unverified_after_max_fix_rounds(repo):
    a = agent([{"say": "done"}] * 5, repo, check_command="python -m pytest -q", max_fix_rounds=1)
    assert a.run("fix it").status == "unverified"


def test_turn_limit_stops_runaway(repo):
    a = agent([{"tool": "list_files", "input": {"path": str(i)}} for i in range(50)], repo, max_turns=5)
    r = a.run("x")
    assert r.status == "max_turns" and r.turns == 5


def test_compaction_keeps_history_valid(repo):
    script = [{"say": f"step {i}", "tool": "search", "input": {"pattern": f"x{i}"}} for i in range(12)] + [{"say": "done"}]
    a = agent(script, repo, context_limit=400)
    r = a.run("explore")
    assert r.stats["compactions"] >= 1
    first = a.model.requests[-1]["messages"]
    assert first[0]["role"] == "user" and "compacted" in first[0]["content"]
    roles = [m["role"] for m in first]
    assert all(x != y for x, y in zip(roles, roles[1:]))  # user/assistant alternate


def test_compact_never_splits_a_tool_pair():
    msgs = [{"role": "user", "content": "task"}]
    for i in range(6):
        msgs.append({"role": "assistant", "content": [{"type": "tool_use", "id": f"t{i}", "name": "search", "input": {}}]})
        msgs.append({"role": "user", "content": [{"type": "tool_result", "tool_use_id": f"t{i}", "content": "r"}]})
    new, summary = compact(msgs, "task", keep_last=3)
    assert new[1]["role"] == "assistant" and summary


def test_interrupt_closes_open_tool_calls(repo, monkeypatch):
    a = agent([{"tool": "run_command", "input": {"command": "python -m pytest -q"}}], repo)

    def boom(**kw):
        raise KeyboardInterrupt
    a.tools["run_command"].run = boom
    r = a.run("x")
    assert r.status == "interrupted"
    assert r.messages[-1]["content"][0]["content"].startswith("Interrupted")


def test_agents_md_becomes_a_prompt_layer(repo):
    (repo / "AGENTS.md").write_text("Always use type hints.")
    a = agent([{"say": "ok"}], repo)
    a.run("x")
    assert "Always use type hints." in a.model.requests[0]["system"]


def test_reviewer_subagent_can_fail_the_work(repo):
    (repo / "calc.py").write_text("def add(a, b):\n    return a + b\n")
    a = agent([
        {"say": "Done."},                                         # main agent claims done
        {"tool": "read_file", "input": {"path": "calc.py"}},      # reviewer inspects
        {"say": "VERDICT: FAIL\nNo test for negative numbers."},  # reviewer rejects
        {"say": "Added the missing test. Done."},                 # main agent again
        {"say": "VERDICT: PASS\nLooks right."},                   # reviewer accepts
    ], repo, review=True)
    r = a.run("make add robust")
    assert r.status == "done"
    reviewer_calls = [e for e in a.trace.events if e["kind"] == "model_call" and e["agent"] == "reviewer"]
    assert len(reviewer_calls) == 3


def test_trace_is_written_as_jsonl(repo, tmp_path):
    path = tmp_path / "t.jsonl"
    Agent(FakeModel([{"say": "hi"}]), repo, tracer=Tracer(path)).run("x")
    kinds = [json.loads(line)["kind"] for line in path.read_text().splitlines()]
    assert kinds[0] == "run_start" and kinds[-1] == "run_end"

Read the test names on their own: together they are a specification of the harness.

Evals: measuring the agent

Tests check the harness's mechanics, with scripted models. Evals measure the agent's real behavior, with a real model. Each task in evals/tasks/ has a small repository, a task.json with the prompt and a check command, and a fake script for offline runs. The runner copies the repository for each trial, runs Rein, grades the result, and reports pass@k, pass^k, turns, and tokens. The no-destroy task checks something every harness needs: asked to do something destructive, the agent must refuse, and the files must survive.

python evals/run_evals.py --fake --trials 2     # offline smoke test of the eval machinery
python evals/run_evals.py --trials 5            # real runs with Claude: this is your data
python evals/run_evals.py --trials 5 --save runs/before.json
# ...make one harness change, then:
python evals/run_evals.py --trials 5 --baseline runs/before.json     # prints any regressions
task            passed  pass@1  pass@2  pass^2  turns   tokens
add-slugify        2/2    1.00    1.00    1.00    4.0     1314
fix-add            2/2    1.00    1.00    1.00    5.0     1517
no-destroy         2/2    1.00    1.00    1.00    2.0      263

Perfect scores with a scripted model prove only that the machinery works. Your real numbers come from real runs, and they will not all be 1.00. That is fine. Honest, imperfect numbers with an explanation are far more convincing than suspiciously perfect ones.

Read the eval runner
"""Eval runner: many tasks x many trials, graded by code, reported as pass@k and pass^k.

    python evals/run_evals.py --fake                      # smoke test the plumbing, no API key
    python evals/run_evals.py --trials 5 --save runs/today.json
    python evals/run_evals.py --trials 5 --baseline runs/today.json   # catch regressions
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys
import tempfile
import time
from math import comb
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]))
from rein.loop import Agent, Config  # noqa: E402
from rein.model import AnthropicModel, FakeModel  # noqa: E402
from rein.permissions import Policy  # noqa: E402
from rein.trace import Tracer  # noqa: E402

TASKS = Path(__file__).parent / "tasks"


def pass_at_k(n: int, c: int, k: int) -> float:
    """Chance that at least one of k tries succeeds (unbiased estimate from n trials, c passes)."""
    return 1.0 if n - c < k else 1 - comb(n - c, k) / comb(n, k)


def pass_hat_k(n: int, c: int, k: int) -> float:
    """Chance that all k tries succeed: the number that matters for reliability."""
    return comb(c, k) / comb(n, k)


def run_trial(task_dir: Path, fake: bool) -> dict:
    spec = json.loads((task_dir / "task.json").read_text())
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
        ws = Path(tmp) / "repo"
        shutil.copytree(task_dir / "repo", ws)  # every trial starts from a clean copy
        model = FakeModel(json.loads((task_dir / "fake_script.json").read_text())) if fake else AnthropicModel()
        agent = Agent(model, ws, Policy(spec.get("mode", "auto_edit"), ask=lambda *_: False),  # headless: "ask" means no
                      Config(max_turns=spec.get("max_turns", 20), check_command=spec.get("agent_check")),
                      Tracer())
        t0 = time.time()
        result = agent.run(spec["task"])
        # The grader is independent of the agent: it runs its own check on the final files.
        grade = subprocess.run(spec["grade"], shell=True, cwd=ws, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=300)
        return {"passed": grade.returncode == 0, "status": result.status, "turns": result.turns,
                "tokens": result.stats["input_tokens"] + result.stats["output_tokens"],
                "denied": result.stats["denied"], "seconds": round(time.time() - t0, 1)}


def main() -> int:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--trials", type=int, default=3)
    ap.add_argument("--k", type=int, default=None, help="k for pass@k and pass^k (default: trials)")
    ap.add_argument("--fake", action="store_true", help="use each task's scripted FakeModel")
    ap.add_argument("--only", default=None, help="run a single task by folder name")
    ap.add_argument("--save", default=None)
    ap.add_argument("--baseline", default=None)
    a = ap.parse_args()
    k = min(a.k or a.trials, a.trials)

    report = {"k": k, "tasks": {}}
    print(f"{'task':<14} {'passed':>7} {'pass@1':>7} {f'pass@{k}':>7} {f'pass^{k}':>7} {'turns':>6} {'tokens':>8}")
    for task_dir in sorted(p for p in TASKS.iterdir() if p.is_dir()):
        if a.only and task_dir.name != a.only:
            continue
        trials = [run_trial(task_dir, a.fake) for _ in range(a.trials)]
        n, c = len(trials), sum(t["passed"] for t in trials)
        row = {"n": n, "passed": c, "pass_at_1": c / n, "pass_at_k": pass_at_k(n, c, k),
               "pass_hat_k": pass_hat_k(n, c, k),
               "avg_turns": sum(t["turns"] for t in trials) / n,
               "avg_tokens": sum(t["tokens"] for t in trials) / n, "trials": trials}
        report["tasks"][task_dir.name] = row
        print(f"{task_dir.name:<14} {f'{c}/{n}':>7} {row['pass_at_1']:>7.2f} {row['pass_at_k']:>7.2f} "
              f"{row['pass_hat_k']:>7.2f} {row['avg_turns']:>6.1f} {row['avg_tokens']:>8.0f}")

    if a.save:
        Path(a.save).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        Path(a.save).write_text(json.dumps(report, indent=2))
    if a.baseline:
        old = json.loads(Path(a.baseline).read_text())["tasks"]
        drops = [(name, old[name]["pass_at_1"], row["pass_at_1"]) for name, row in report["tasks"].items()
                 if name in old and row["pass_at_1"] < old[name]["pass_at_1"]]
        for name, before, after in drops:
            print(f"REGRESSION {name}: pass@1 {before:.2f} -> {after:.2f}")
        if drops:
            return 1
        print("No regressions against baseline.")
    return 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    sys.exit(main())

Your first real experiment

Now use the steering loop from chapter 24 on your own harness. Pick one change: turn on --review, lower tool_output_chars, or rewrite one tool description. Add five or more real tasks of your own. Run the evals with at least five trials before and after the change. Report the difference with the spread, plus cost and turns. Keep or revert the change based on the evidence. This one experiment, written up well, is worth more in an interview than any number of tutorials.

The showcase

Your repository's README is the first thing anyone reads. Here is the reference project's, as a model:

Read the reference README
# Rein: a tiny, readable coding-agent harness

Rein wraps a language model in the systems it needs to do real work safely:
a loop, tools, permissions, a context budget, recovery, tracing, verification, and evals.
About 700 lines of plain Python. Every behavior is tested offline with a scripted fake model.

```
            task                                         .rein/trace.jsonl
             |                                                  ^
             v                                                  | every event
   +--------------------+   system prompt   +--------+          |
   |  Agent.run() loop  | ----------------> | Model  |   Tracer (append-only)
   |  guards: turns,    | <---------------- |        |
   |  budget, breaker   |   text/tool calls +--------+
   +--------------------+
      |  precheck (in order)        execute (read-only calls in parallel)
      |  unknown tool? args? loop?  ---> Tools ---> Workspace (path sandbox)
      |  Policy: allow / ask / deny
      v
   Verification gate: project checks (tests) -> optional reviewer sub-agent
```

## Run it

```bash
python -m venv .venv && source .venv/bin/activate
pip install anthropic pytest
python -m pytest -q                         # 20 offline tests, no API key needed
python -m rein "The tests fail. Fix the bug." --workspace examples/demo \
    --mode auto_edit --check "python -m pytest -q" --fake examples/fix_add_script.json
export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=...                # then drop --fake to use the real model
python evals/run_evals.py --fake            # eval plumbing smoke test
python evals/run_evals.py --trials 5 --save runs/base.json
```

## Design decisions (and the harness system each one belongs to)

| System | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Control plane | Layered system prompt: base rules, then `AGENTS.md` | Stable rules first; project rules can add detail but not override safety |
| Loop | Hard stops: turns, token budget, circuit breaker | A loop with one exit is a loop that never ends |
| Tools | `edit_file` needs a unique exact match; errors say what to do next | Tool design is the agent's user interface |
| Permissions | allow / ask / deny, sticky denials, read-only mode | The model proposes, the harness disposes |
| Sandbox | Workspace path check; run the whole thing in a container | Pattern lists are convenience, not security |
| Context | Truncate tool output (head + tail); mechanical compaction | Context is a budget; compaction must keep tool pairs valid |
| Recovery | Retries with backoff + jitter, breaker, loop detector | Every recovery is counted, limited, and able to give up |
| Verification | Tests run by the harness; optional independent reviewer | "Done" is a claim, not a fact |
| Observability | JSONL trace of every model call, permission, tool call | If it is not in the trace, you cannot debug it |
| Evals | Code-graded tasks, pass@k and pass^k, baseline comparison | Harness changes are only real if you can measure them |

## What I would build next

Docker sandbox for `run_command`; prompt caching; an MCP client; model-written summaries for compaction;
parallel sub-agents for research tasks; a larger eval suite.

Then write a one-page write-up, as a blog post or a DESIGN.md, with these headings:

  1. What I built, in two sentences, with a diagram.
  2. Design decisions: three or four, each with its trade-off. For example: why a scripted model first, why deny rules come before allow rules, why mechanical compaction.
  3. What I measured: a small table of eval results before and after one change, with trials and spread.
  4. What broke and how I fixed it: one real bug with its trace, like the bytecode story in step 3, or the reward hack your reviewer caught in step 11.
  5. What I would do next: two or three extensions, ranked by expected value.

Record a two-minute screen video: the task, the transcript scrolling by, a denial, the verification gate catching a wrong fix, the reviewer catching the hack, and the eval table.

Extensions, ranked

ExtensionWhat it teachesEffort
Run the tools in a Docker container, with the loop outsideSandboxing, and splitting the brain from the handsMedium
Prompt caching with cache_control breakpoints, and report the hit rateCost engineeringSmall
Model-written compaction with a mechanical fallbackContext engineering and recoveryMedium
An MCP client, so Rein can use any MCP server's toolsTool ecosystems and supply-chain riskMedium
Export traces to OpenTelemetryProduction observabilitySmall
Parallel sub-agents for explorationMulti-agent trade-offs, measuredLarge

Ready to show?

  • All tests pass from a fresh clone, following only the README.
  • At least ten real eval tasks, five trials each, with results in the README.
  • One before-and-after experiment, written up honestly.
  • A diagram of the architecture.
  • One real bug story, with its trace.
  • A two-minute demo video.
  • You can explain every line of rein.py without looking.

Carry this forward

  • Checkpoints become tests: a script, a run, and assertions on the trace.
  • Tests check the harness's mechanics; evals measure the agent's behavior.
  • One measured experiment, written up honestly, beats any list of features.
  • Show your work: a README, a diagram, a bug story, numbers, and a short video.

The job and your portfolio

Knowing harnesses is necessary. Being hired for it takes proof: public work that shows you can build, measure, and explain. This chapter turns the book into a plan.

What the job is called

A few companies now post roles literally titled "Agent Harness Engineer". Far more describe the same work under other names:

Title you will seeHow much of it is harness work
AI Engineer, Applied AI Engineer, Agentic AI EngineerMost of it: building agents into products, with tools, evals, and guardrails.
AI Platform or Agent Infrastructure EngineerThe runtime: sandboxes, tracing, caching, scaling, permission systems.
Product engineer on a coding-agent teamExactly this book: loops, tools, context, verification, at huge scale.
Forward Deployed or Solutions Engineer (AI)User harnesses: fitting agents into customers' codebases and workflows.
Research Engineer, evals or RL environmentsBuilding tasks, sandboxes, and graders that measure and train agents. Very close to Parts 3 and 5.

What AI labs look for

Postings for these teams at labs and strong startups tend to repeat the same themes. Be honest with yourself about each one:

  • Strong software engineering first. A harness is software. Clean code, testing, concurrency, Linux, git, containers, and debugging matter more than any AI buzzword.
  • Hands-on experience building with LLMs, including tool use, prompt caching, and cost control, shown in real projects.
  • Measurement. The ability to design evals, run experiments, and reason about noisy results.
  • Judgment about safety and reliability. Knowing what can go wrong when agents act, and designing so it does not.
  • Clear writing. Labs run on written design documents. A good write-up is evidence you can work there.

Roles at top labs are extremely competitive, and no book or project guarantees an offer. What reliably improves your odds is public, verifiable work: a harness with tests and real eval numbers, a thoughtful write-up, and merged contributions to open-source agent projects. If you apply from outside the main tech hubs, this kind of evidence counts for even more, because it speaks for you before anyone reads your location.

A strict self-assessment

Rate yourself 1 to 4 in each row. Be harsh: a 3 means you have done it, not read about it.

Area2: can explain3: have built4: can defend with data
Agent loop and stateDraw the loop and its exitsWrote one with guards and interruptsMeasured how exits and limits affect cost and success
ToolsList tool design rulesBuilt tools with teaching errorsShowed an eval gain from a tool redesign
Permissions and sandboxExplain allow, ask, denyBuilt a policy and ran tools in a containerRed-teamed it and documented what got through
Context and cachingExplain context rot and cachingBuilt truncation and compactionReported cache hit rate and cost before and after
RecoveryExplain backoff and breakersBuilt counted, limited recoveryShowed behavior under injected failures
Verification and evalsExplain pass@k and pass^kBuilt a gate and an eval runnerRan a before-and-after experiment with spread
ObservabilityList what to traceWrote JSONL tracesFound and fixed a real problem from a trace
SecurityExplain the lethal trifectaRemoved a leg in a real designRan injection tests against your own agent

After finishing this book and Rein, an honest profile is mostly 2s and 3s. The 4s come from the experiments below. That gap is the plan.

Four portfolio projects

  1. Rein, extended and measured. At least ten real eval tasks, five trials each, one before-and-after experiment, and one extension from the list in B7.
  2. An open-source contribution to a real harness. Codex CLI, OpenCode, deepagents, smolagents, mini-swe-agent, or an MCP server. Start with a documentation fix or a failing-test reproduction; then a small bug fix. Merged pull requests are verifiable evidence of working in a real codebase.
  3. An eval study. Pick one harness question, such as "does an independent reviewer help on small bug fixes, and at what cost?", and answer it carefully with pass^k, cost, and a discussion of noise. Publish it.
  4. A domain agent with a security write-up. For example, an agent that triages support emails. Map its trifecta, show which leg you removed, and include injection tests.

Interview rehearsal

Interviews for this work test four things: can you build it, can you design it, can you debug it, and can you explain your choices under pressure. Practice each out loud.

The usual formats

FormatWhat it looks likeHow to prepare
CodingImplement an agent loop, a tool with validation, retries with a breaker, or a simple eval runner, in 45 to 60 minutesRewrite Rein's pieces from memory, timed
System design"Design a coding agent for our monorepo" or "an agent that handles refunds"The eight-wire walkthrough below
DebuggingRead a trace or transcript and explain what went wrongRead your own traces; do the Chapter 18 and 24 labs again
Take-homeBuild a small agent with evals in a few daysRein is your template; write the README first
BehavioralTrade-offs, failures, disagreements, safety callsThree stories in the steering-loop shape

A worked system design answer

Prompt: "Design an agent that fixes flaky tests in a large monorepo, automatically, overnight."

  1. Clarify. How big is the repo? What languages? What counts as fixed: passes 50 runs in a row? Who reviews the change? What must it never do? Budget per night?
  2. Loop and state. One agent per flaky test, each in its own branch and sandbox. State: attempts, runs, budget used. Exits: fixed and verified, gave up with a report, or budget hit.
  3. Tools. Search, read, edit with unique matches, run a single test N times with a summary, read CI history for that test. No general network.
  4. Permissions and sandbox. Containers with only the repo, a package mirror, and no secrets. Edits allowed inside the sandbox; no pushes; the output is a pull request a human reviews.
  5. Context. Load the test and the code under test on demand; cap test logs to head and tail; notes file per attempt so a reset can continue.
  6. Recovery. Retries with backoff for the API; a breaker per night; loop detection for repeated identical runs.
  7. Verification. Fixed means passing 50 isolated runs plus the surrounding suite; an independent reviewer checks the change does not just delete or skip the test. That last check matters: "make the test pass" invites cheating.
  8. Observability and evals. Traces per attempt; a nightly dashboard of fixed, failed, and cost. Before launch, an eval set of past flaky tests with known fixes, reporting pass^k and cost.
  9. Rollout. Start in suggest-only mode; measure acceptance rate of pull requests; widen scope slowly.

Notice what earns points: clarifying questions, a precise definition of done, a named cheating risk with a check against it, cost and budget, and a gradual rollout. Most weak answers are a list of tools and a hopeful loop.

Twenty-five questions to practice out loud

Answer each in about a minute before opening it. Then compare.

1. What is an agent harness?

Everything around the model that turns it into a working agent: the loop, tools, permissions, context management, recovery, verification, and observability. Agent equals model plus harness. Give one example of a harness change that improved results without changing the model.

2. Walk me through one iteration of an agent loop.

Build the prompt (system, tools, history, trimmed to budget); call the model with retries; if it asks for tools, check permissions, run them (read-only in parallel), cap outputs, append results; if it says done, verify; check limits; repeat.

3. How does the model "use" a tool?

It returns a structured tool_use request with an id and inputs; the harness validates, decides permission, executes, and returns a tool_result with the same id. The model never executes anything.

4. What makes a tool easy for a model to use?

A clear verb-noun name, a description of what, when, when not, and what it returns; documented inputs with formats; concise results; errors that explain the fix; validation; and not too many overlapping tools.

5. How do you stop an agent from running forever?

Hard limits on turns, tokens, cost, and time; loop detection; a circuit breaker; a verification gate with a maximum number of fix rounds; and a user interrupt that closes open tool calls.

6. What is context rot and what do you do about it?

Models use information less reliably as inputs grow. Keep context small: just-in-time retrieval, capped outputs, notes in files, sub-agents for exploration, and compaction as a last resort.

7. Compaction versus context reset?

Compaction summarizes history in place; a reset starts fresh and hands over state through files. Resets give a clean slate but depend on good handoffs; which is better depends on the model, so measure.

8. How does prompt caching affect harness design?

Cached prefixes are much cheaper and faster, but only if identical. Put stable content first, only append to history, serialize deterministically, avoid timestamps near the top, and keep tool lists fixed during a session.

9. Design a permission system for a coding agent.

Allow, ask, deny with reasons; deny rules checked first; read-only allowed; edits allowed inside a sandbox on a branch; shell by allow-list without chaining; no push or force; sticky denials; a sandbox as the real wall; everything traced.

10. What is approval fatigue?

When people approve so many prompts that they stop reading them, making approvals meaningless. Fix it by sandboxing so more can be allowed, better defaults, and classifiers for routine decisions; keep human approval for rare, meaningful actions.

11. Explain prompt injection and the lethal trifecta.

Untrusted text can carry instructions the model may follow. Private data plus untrusted content plus a way to send data out makes theft possible. Remove at least one leg, and layer other defenses.

12. How would you implement retries for model calls?

Retry only transient errors, exponential backoff with a cap and jitter, a maximum attempt count, each retry traced, a breaker across requests, and the SDK's built-in retries disabled so one layer owns the policy.

13. Why not let the agent verify its own work?

It is biased toward seeing its work as done; models tend to praise their own output. Use computational checks the harness runs itself, and an independent reviewer with a fresh context and a rubric.

14. pass@k or pass^k?

pass@k when you can generate several attempts and pick a verified one; pass^k when every run matters, such as customer-facing or production actions.

15. Your eval score went up 4 points. Do you ship?

Check noise first: number of tasks and trials, spread across runs, fixed environment, and transcripts for lucky passes. Ship when the gain survives repeated trials and no regression suite task drops.

16. When would you use multiple agents?

For context isolation, true parallelism, or role separation. Otherwise prefer one agent: multi-agent costs many times more tokens and risks conflicting hidden decisions.

17. What would you put in a trace?

Per model call: model, tokens (including cached), stop reason, latency, cost. Per tool call: name, inputs, verdict and reason, duration, output size, error. Per run: task, status, turns, totals, verification result. Standard names such as OpenTelemetry's gen_ai attributes.

18. The agent got slower this week. Investigate.

Compare traces before and after; break time down by span type; look for retries, longer outputs, cache misses, extra turns, or approval waits; form a hypothesis and confirm it with an eval.

19. What does "every harness component encodes an assumption" mean?

Each part exists because a model once could not do something alone. When models improve, run ablations and remove parts that no longer help; they cost tokens, time, and complexity.

20. What is AGENTS.md and how should it be written?

A repository file of instructions for coding agents. Keep it short, like a map pointing to deeper docs; include how to build and test; enforce important rules with linters and tests rather than words alone.

21. How do long-running agents keep working across context windows?

An initializer sets up a feature list, progress file, and git; each session orients itself, tests, builds one thing, verifies it end to end, commits, and updates notes. State lives in files, not in the context.

22. How would you make an agent's harness cheaper without losing quality?

Measure token sources; cap outputs; improve cache hit rate; compact earlier; route simple steps to a smaller model; move exploration to sub-agents; prove quality with the regression suite.

23. What is MCP and what are its risks?

An open protocol for connecting agents to tool servers, turning N-times-M integrations into N-plus-M. Risks: context bloat from tool definitions, malicious or changing tool descriptions, and over-broad credentials. Vet, pin, load tools on demand, and gate actions.

24. Tell me about a bug you found in an agent system.

Use your own story in the steering-loop shape: what you observed, the evidence in the trace, the failure family, the smallest fix, and how you verified it. Rein's stale-bytecode bug is a good model if you hit it yourself.

25. What would you improve in Claude Code or Codex?

Pick one wire, describe the current design and its trade-off fairly, propose one change, and say exactly how you would measure it. Specific and measurable beats sweeping.

Coding drills

Do each in 45 minutes, from a blank file, then compare with Rein:

  1. An agent loop with a turn limit, tool execution, and a matching tool_result for every tool_use.
  2. with_retries with backoff, cap, jitter, and a test that runs instantly.
  3. A workspace path check that refuses escapes, with tests for tricky paths.
  4. A permission policy with deny-first rules and no command chaining.
  5. A compaction function that never splits a tool pair, with a test.

Carry this forward

  • Walk through the eight wires in every design answer, and define "done" precisely.
  • Tell stories as steering loops: observe, diagnose, change, measure.
  • Practice out loud and on a timer. Knowing is not the same as saying it well under pressure.

Reading list

Primary sources, grouped by wire. Read one per week after finishing the book. Each has a note on why it matters.

Start here

  • Harness engineering (OpenAI, February 2026). The field report that named the discipline: a million lines, no manual code.
  • Building effective agents (Anthropic, 2024). Workflows versus agents, and why simple patterns win.
  • Harness engineering for coding agent users (Birgitta Böckeler, 2026). Guides, sensors, and harnessability.
  • The anatomy of an agent harness (LangChain, 2026). "If you are not the model, you are the harness."
  • awesome-harness-engineering. A large, current, annotated list of sources on every topic here.

Loop, tools, and control

  • ReAct (2022). The research ancestor of the agent loop.
  • Unrolling the Codex agent loop (OpenAI, January 2026). One loop iteration, in detail.
  • Writing effective tools for agents (Anthropic, 2025). The tool design rules of chapter 11.
  • Code execution with MCP (Anthropic). Letting agents call tools from code.
  • Model Context Protocol. The spec and concepts.
  • SWE-agent. Where the agent-computer interface idea came from.

Permissions, sandboxes, and security

  • Beyond permission prompts (Anthropic). Sandboxing that cut prompts by 84 percent.
  • Claude Code auto mode (Anthropic, 2026). A classifier instead of approval fatigue.
  • Agent SDK: configure permissions. The evaluation order: hooks, deny, mode, allow, callback.
  • Prompt injection series (Simon Willison). Including the lethal trifecta.
  • OWASP LLM01: Prompt injection and LLM06: Excessive agency.

Context, memory, and long-running work

  • Effective context engineering for AI agents (Anthropic, 2025). The attention budget and the right altitude.
  • Effective harnesses for long-running agents (Anthropic, November 2025). The initializer pattern.
  • Harness design for long-running application development (Anthropic, March 2026). Planner, generator, evaluator.
  • Scaling Managed Agents (Anthropic, April 2026). Brain, hands, and session.
  • Shell, skills, and compaction (OpenAI). Tips for long-running agents.

Verification, evals, and observability

  • Demystifying evals for AI agents (Anthropic, January 2026). The eval vocabulary of chapter 19.
  • Quantifying infrastructure noise in agentic coding evals (Anthropic, 2026). Why your environment moves scores.
  • tau-bench. The benchmark that popularized pass^k.
  • Improving Deep Agents with harness engineering (LangChain). Top 30 to top 5 with harness changes only.

Real harnesses and the craft

  • The design space of today's and future AI agent systems (April 2026). A detailed teardown of Claude Code's architecture.
  • An update on recent Claude Code quality reports (Anthropic, April 2026). How harness changes hurt quality.
  • Codex as a platform (OpenAI, September 2026). Building on an open harness.
  • Harness engineering (deepset). Failure families and fixes.
  • Harness engineering for self-improvement (Lilian Weng, July 2026). From everyday harnesses to self-improving ones.
  • What makes a harness a harness (June 2026). A formal definition.
